**Vietnam**,[^cite_note-12] officially the **Socialist Republic of Viet Nam** (**SRV**),[^cite_note-13] is a country at the eastern edge of [Mainland Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainland_Southeast_Asia). With an area of about 331,000 square kilometres and a population of over 102 million, it is the world's [16th-most populous country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population). One of two [communist states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_state) in [Southeast Asia](28741.md),[^cite_note-14] Vietnam is bordered by [China](5405.md) to the north, and [Laos](17752.md) and [Cambodia](334751.md) to the west; it lies along the [Gulf of Thailand](198107.md) to the southwest and the [South China Sea](74209.md) to the east, where it has shared and [disputed maritime borders](32005536.md) with other countries. Its capital is [Hanoi](56667.md), while its [largest city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Vietnam#Largest_cities_by_population) is [Ho Chi Minh City](66956.md).

Vietnam was inhabited by the [Paleolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleolithic) age, with states established in the first millennium BC on the [Red River Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_River_Delta) in modern-day [northern Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Vietnam).[^cite_note-15] The [Han dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_dynasty) annexed northern and central Vietnam, which were subsequently under [Chinese rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_under_Chinese_rule) from 111 BC until the [first dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ng%C3%B4_dynasty) emerged in 939. Successive [monarchical dynasties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Vietnamese_dynasties) absorbed Chinese influences through [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism) and [Buddhism](3267529.md), and [expanded southward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nam_ti%E1%BA%BFn) to the [Mekong Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mekong_Delta), conquering [Champa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champa). During most of the 17th and 18th centuries, Vietnam was effectively divided into two domains, [Đàng Trong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%A0ng_Trong) and [Đàng Ngoài](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%A0ng_Ngo%C3%A0i). The [Nguyễn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_dynasty)—the last imperial dynasty—surrendered to [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Third_Republic) in 1883. In 1887, its territory was integrated into [French Indochina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Indochina) as three separate regions. In the immediate [aftermath of World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aftermath_of_World_War_II), the communist-led [Viet Minh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viet_Minh) coalition front launched the [August Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_Revolution) and [declared](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_independence_of_the_Democratic_Republic_of_Vietnam) Vietnam's independence from the [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) in 1945.

Vietnam went through [prolonged warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indochina_wars) in the 20th century. After [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Indochina_in_World_War_II), France returned to reassert colonial control in the [First Indochina War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Indochina_War), but was defeated in 1954 amid the global [Cold War](325329.md). As a result of the [accords signed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Geneva_Conference) between the Viet Minh and France, Vietnam was also separated into two parts. The [Vietnam War](32611.md) later broke out between the communist [North Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Vietnam), supported by the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) and [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_in_the_Vietnam_War), and the anti-communist [South Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Vietnam), supported by the [United States](8453309.md). Upon the [North Vietnamese victory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Saigon) in 1975, Vietnam reunified as a [communist state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_state) that self-designated as a [socialist state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_state_\(communism\)) under the [Communist Party of Vietnam](400546.md) (CPV) in 1976.&#x20;

An ineffective [planned economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned_economy), a trade embargo by the [West](21208200.md), and wars with [Cambodia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambodian%E2%80%93Vietnamese_War) and [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-Vietnamese_War) crippled the country further. In 1986, the CPV launched [economic and political reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%E1%BB%95i_M%E1%BB%9Bi) similar to the [Chinese economic reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_and_opening_up), transforming the country to a [socialist-oriented market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist-oriented_market_economy). The reforms facilitated Vietnamese reintegration into the [global economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_economy) and [politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_politics). Vietnam is a [developing country](78449.md) with an upper-middle–income economy.[^cite_note-16] It has [high levels of corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_in_Vietnam), [censorship](52301471.md), [environmental issues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_Vietnam), and a poor [human rights record](4321666.md). It is part of international and intergovernmental institutions, including the [ASEAN](106539.md), the [APEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/APEC), the [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement), the [OIF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie), and the [WTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization). It has assumed a seat on the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) twice.

## Etymology

Main article: [Names of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_Vietnam)

The name *Việt Nam* (pronounced [\[viə̂tˀ nāːm\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Vietnamese), [chữ Hán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%E1%BB%AF_H%C3%A1n): [越南](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E8%B6%8A%E5%8D%97)), literally "Viet South", means per Vietnamese word order "Viet of the South";[^cite_note-Brindley_2015_p27-17] meanwhile, per [Classical Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Chinese) word order, "Việt Nam" ambiguously means either "South of the Viet"[^cite_note-Brindley_2015_p27-17][^cite_note-AC-18] or "Viet people's Southern country".[^cite_note-AC-18]

A variation of the name, [Nanyue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanyue) (or Nam Việt, [南越](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E5%8D%97%E8%B6%8A)), was first documented in the 2nd century BC.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoods200238-19] The term "[Việt](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Vi%E1%BB%87t)" (Yue) ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language): [越](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E8%B6%8A); [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Yuè*; [Cantonese Yale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonese_Yale): *Yuht*; [Wade–Giles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wade%E2%80%93Giles): *Yüeh<sup>4</sup>*; [Vietnamese](32511.md): *[Việt](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Vi%E1%BB%87t)*) in [Early Middle Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_Middle_Chinese) was first written using the [logograph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logogram) "戉" for an axe (a homophone), in [oracle bone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracle_bone) and bronze inscriptions of the [Shang dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shang_dynasty) (c. 1200 BC), and later as "越".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENormanMei1976-20] It referred to a people or chieftain to the northwest of the Shang.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeacham1996-21] In the 8th century BC, a tribe on the middle [Yangtze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangtze) were called the [Yangyue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangyue), a term later used for peoples further south.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeacham1996-21][^cite_note-23] Between the 7th and 4th centuries BC, 'Yue'/'Việt' referred to the [State of Yue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yue_\(state\)) in the lower Yangtze basin and its people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENormanMei1976-20][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeacham1996-21] From the 3rd century BC, the term was used for the non-Chinese populations of southern China and northern Vietnam, with particular ethnic groups called [Minyue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minyue), [Ouyue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ouyue), Luoyue (Vietnamese: [Lạc Việt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%E1%BA%A1c_Vi%E1%BB%87t)), etc., collectively called the [Baiyue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baiyue) (Bách Việt, [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language): [百越](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E7%99%BE%E8%B6%8A); [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Bǎiyuè*; [Cantonese Yale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonese_Yale): *Baak Yuet*; [Vietnamese](32511.md): *Bách Việt*; [lit.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literal_translation) 'Hundred Yue/Viet').[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENormanMei1976-20][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeacham1996-21][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYue_Hashimoto19721-24] The term 'Baiyue'/'Bách Việt' first appeared in the book *[Lüshi Chunqiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%BCshi_Chunqiu)* compiled around 239 BC.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKnoblockRiegel2001510-25] By the 17th and 18th centuries AD, educated Vietnamese apparently referred to themselves as *người Việt* (Viet people) or *người Nam* (southern people).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELieberman2003405-26]

The form *Việt Nam* ([越南](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E8%B6%8A%E5%8D%97)) is first recorded in the 16th-century oracular poem *[Sấm Trạng Trình](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%E1%BA%A5m_Tr%E1%BA%A1ng_Tr%C3%ACnh)*. The name has also been found on 12 [steles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stele) carved in the 16th and 17th centuries, including one at Bao Lam Pagoda in [Hải Phòng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haiphong) that dates to 1558.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPhan1976510-27] In 1802, Nguyễn Phúc Ánh, who became Emperor [Gia Long](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gia_Long), established the [Nguyễn dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_dynasty). Gia Long asked the [Jiaqing Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jiaqing_Emperor) of the [Qing dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty) to confer on him the title 'King of Nam Việt / Nanyue' (南越 in Chinese character) after seizing power in Annam. Emperor Jiaqing refused because the name was related to [Zhao Tuo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhao_Tuo)'s Nanyue, which included the regions of [Guangxi](47836950.md) and [Guangdong](65109.md) in southern China, so he decided to call the area "Việt Nam" instead,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoods200238-19][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOoi2004932-28][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaofeiGuoqing2016-29] meaning per [Classical Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Chinese) word order "land to the south of the Viet land" ([Vietnamese](32511.md): *vùng đất phía Nam đất Việt*). However, the Vietnamese just understood "Việt Nam" as either, also per Classical Chinese word-order, "Viet people's Southern country" (Vietnamese: *nước Nam của người Việt*)[^cite_note-AC-18] or even per Vietnamese word order, "Viet of the South".[^cite_note-Brindley_2015_p27-17] Between 1804-13, the name Vietnam was used officially by Emperor Gia Long. It was revived in the 20th century in [Phan Bội Châu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phan_B%E1%BB%99i_Ch%C3%A2u)'s *[History of the Loss of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Loss_of_Vietnam)*, and by the [Vietnamese Nationalist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vi%E1%BB%87t_Nam_Qu%E1%BB%91c_D%C3%A2n_%C4%90%E1%BA%A3ng) (VNQDĐ).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETonnessonAntlov1996117-30] The country was usually called *Annam* and its people *Annamites* until 1945, when the [imperial government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Vietnam) in [Huế](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hu%E1%BA%BF) adopted *Việt Nam*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETonnessonAntlov1996126-31]

## History

Main article: [History of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Vietnam)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Vietnamese history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Vietnamese_history).

### Prehistory and early history

Archaeological excavations have revealed the existence of humans in what is now Vietnam as early as the [Paleolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleolithic) age. Stone artefacts excavated in [Gia Lai province](2542951.md) have been claimed to date to 780,000 years ago,[^cite_note-32] based on associated find of [tektites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tektite), however this claim has been challenged because tektites are often found in archaeological sites of various ages in Vietnam.[^cite_note-33] *[Homo erectus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homo_erectus)* fossils dating to around 500,000 BC have been found in caves in [Lạng Sơn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%E1%BA%A1ng_S%C6%A1n_province) and [Nghệ An](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngh%E1%BB%87_An_province) provinces in northern Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcKinney2009-34] The oldest *[Homo sapiens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homo_sapiens)* fossils from [Mainland Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainland_Southeast_Asia) are of [Middle Pleistocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Pleistocene) provenance, and include isolated tooth fragments from Tham Om and Hang Hum.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAkazawaAokiKimura1992321-35][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERabett2012109-36][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDennellPorr201441-37] Teeth attributed to *Homo sapiens* from the [Late Pleistocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Pleistocene) have been found at Dong Can,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMatsumuraYonedaYukioOxenham200812-38] and from the Early [Holocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocene) at Mai Da Dieu,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMatsumuraLan_CuongKim_ThuyAnezaki2001-39][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOxenhamTayles200636-40] Lang Gao[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENguyen198516-41][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKarlstromKallen200283-42] and Lang Cuom.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOxenhamBuckley2015329-43] Areas comprising what is now Vietnam participated in the Maritime Jade Road, as ascertained by archaeological research.[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45][^cite_note-46][^cite_note-47]

By about 1,000 BC, the development of wet-[rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) cultivation in the [Ma River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma_River) and [Red River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_River_\(Asia\)) floodplains led to the flourishing of [Đông Sơn culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%B4ng_S%C6%A1n_culture),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHigham1984-48][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENang_ChungGiang_Hai201731-49] notable for its [bronze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronze) casting used to make elaborate bronze [Đông Sơn drums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%B4ng_S%C6%A1n_drums).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEde_LaetHerrmann1996408-50][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECalo200951-51][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKiernan201731-52] This culture spread to the rest of [Southeast Asia](28741.md), including [Maritime Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_Southeast_Asia), throughout the first millennium BC.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECalo200951-51][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECookeLiAnderson201146-53]

### Dynastic Vietnam

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Vietnam under Chinese rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Vietnam_under_Chinese_rule).



[Đại Việt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%E1%BA%A1i_Vi%E1%BB%87t), [Champa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champa), [Angkor Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmer_Empire) and their neighbours, late 13th century



Vietnam's territories around 1838, during the [Vietnamese occupation of Cambodia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_invasions_of_Cambodia)

According to Vietnamese legends, the [Hồng Bàng dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%E1%BB%93ng_B%C3%A0ng_dynasty) of the [Hùng kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%B9ng_kings), first established in 2879 BC, is considered the first state established in Vietnam (then known as Xích Quỷ and later [Văn Lang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C4%83n_Lang)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPelley2002151-54] Văn Lang was established by [Lạc Việt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%E1%BA%A1c_Vi%E1%BB%87t) tribes,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKiernan201753-55] who were likely a confederacy of multilingual [Austroasiatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austroasiatic_languages) and [Kra-Dai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kra-Dai_languages) speakers who occupied the [Red River Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_River_Delta) in northern Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKiernan201742-56][^cite_note-57]

In 257 BC, the last Hùng king was defeated by Thục Phán. He consolidated the [Lạc Việt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%E1%BA%A1c_Vi%E1%BB%87t) and [Âu Việt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%82u_Vi%E1%BB%87t) tribes, who came from southern China,[^cite_note-58] to form the [Âu Lạc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%82u_L%E1%BA%A1c), proclaiming himself [An Dương Vương](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An_D%C6%B0%C6%A1ng_V%C6%B0%C6%A1ng).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuc_TranThu_Ha20008-59]

In 179 BC, a Chinese general named [Zhao Tuo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhao_Tuo) (Triệu Đà) defeated An Dương Vương and consolidated Âu Lạc into [Nanyue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanyue).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENang_ChungGiang_Hai201731-49] However, Nanyue was itself [incorporated into the empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southward_expansion_of_the_Han_dynasty) of the Chinese [Han dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_dynasty) in 111 BC after the [Han–Nanyue War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han%E2%80%93Nanyue_War).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOoi2004932-28][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYao201662-60] Through Han rule, [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism) was introduced into the region and came to influence Vietnamese societal norms and governance.[^cite_note-61] For the next thousand years, what is now northern Vietnam remained mostly under [Chinese rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_under_Chinese_rule).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmgren1980-62][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor198330-63][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButtinger196818,_Ch.2-64] Early independence movements, such as those of the [Trưng Sisters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tr%C6%B0ng_Sisters) and [Lady Triệu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Tri%E1%BB%87u),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPelley2002177-65] were temporarily successful,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECottrell200915-66] though the region gained a longer period of independence as Vạn Xuân under the [Anterior Lý dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anterior_L%C3%BD_dynasty) between AD 544 and 602.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThai_NguyenMung_Nguyen195833-67][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChesneaux196620-68][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUniversity_of_California197224-69] By the early 10th century, northern Vietnam had gained autonomy, but not sovereignty, under the [Khúc family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kh%C3%BAc_family).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETuyet_TranReid200632-70]

In AD 938, the Vietnamese lord [Ngô Quyền](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ng%C3%B4_Quy%E1%BB%81n) defeated the forces of the Chinese [Southern Han](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Han) state at [Bạch Đằng River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_B%E1%BA%A1ch_%C4%90%E1%BA%B1ng_\(938\)) and achieved full independence for Vietnam in 939 after a millennium of Chinese domination.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHien_Le200365-71][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHong_LienSharrock201455-72][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKiernan2017226-73] By the 960s, the dynastic [Đại Việt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%E1%BA%A1i_Vi%E1%BB%87t) (*Great Viet*) kingdom was established, and Vietnamese society enjoyed a golden era under the Lý and [Trần](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tr%E1%BA%A7n_dynasty) dynasties. During the rule of the Trần dynasty, Đại Việt repelled three [Mongol invasions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_invasions_of_Vietnam).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECottrell200916-74][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHong_LienSharrock201495-75] Meanwhile, the [Mahāyāna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mah%C4%81y%C4%81na) branch of [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Vietnam) flourished and became the state religion.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKiernan2017226-73][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeyes1995183-76] Following the 1406–7 [Ming–Hồ War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming%E2%80%93H%E1%BB%93_War), which overthrew the [Hồ dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%E1%BB%93_dynasty), Vietnamese independence was [interrupted briefly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Chinese_domination_of_Vietnam) by the Chinese [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty), but was restored by [Lê Lợi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%AA_L%E1%BB%A3i), the founder of the [Lê dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%AA_dynasty).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHong_LienSharrock2014111-77] The Vietnamese polity reached their zenith in the Lê dynasty of the 15th century, especially during the reign of emperor [Lê Thánh Tông](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%AA_Th%C3%A1nh_T%C3%B4ng) (1460–1497).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHong_LienSharrock2014120-78][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKiernan2017265-79] Between the 11th and 18th centuries, the Vietnamese polity expanded southward in a gradual process known as *[Nam tiến](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nam_ti%E1%BA%BFn)* ("Southward expansion"),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndersonWhitmore2014158-80] eventually conquering the kingdom of [Champa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champa) and part of the [Khmer Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-Angkor_Period).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVo201113-81][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOoiAnh_Tuan2015212-82][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPhuong_Linh201639-83]

From the 16th century onward, civil strife and frequent political infighting engulfed much of Đại Việt. First, the Chinese-supported [Mạc dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%E1%BA%A1c_dynasty) challenged the Lê dynasty's power.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndersonWhitmore2014174-84] After the Mạc dynasty was defeated, the Lê dynasty was nominally reinstalled. Actual power, however, was divided between the northern [Trịnh lords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tr%E1%BB%8Bnh_lords) and the southern [Nguyễn lords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_lords), who engaged in a [civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tr%E1%BB%8Bnh%E2%80%93Nguy%E1%BB%85n_War) for more than four decades before a truce was called in the 1670s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeonard1984131-85] Vietnam was divided into North (Trịnh) and South (Nguyễn) from 1600 to 1777. During this period, the Nguyễn expanded into the [Mekong Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mekong_Delta), annexing the [Central Highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Highlands_\(Vietnam\)) and the Khmer lands in the Mekong Delta.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVo201113-81][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPhuong_Linh201639-83][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOoi2004356-86] The division of the country ended a century later when the [Tây Sơn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A2y_S%C6%A1n) brothers helped Trịnh to end Nguyễn; they also established a new dynasty and ended Trịnh. However, their rule did not last long, and they were defeated by the remnants of the Nguyễn lords, led by [Nguyễn Ánh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gia_Long). Nguyễn Ánh unified Vietnam, and established the [Nguyễn dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_dynasty), ruling under the name Gia Long.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOoi2004356-86]

### French Indochina

Further information: [Cochinchina campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochinchina_campaign), [Tonkin campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonkin_campaign), [Sino-French War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-French_War), and [French Indochina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Indochina)



[Capture of Saigon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Saigon) by [Charles Rigault de Genouilly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Rigault_de_Genouilly) on 18 February 1859

Photograph of the Grand Palais building in Hanoi

The [Grand Palais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Palais_\(Hanoi\)) built for the [1902–1903 world's fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanoi_exhibition), when [Hanoi](56667.md) became French Indochina's capital

In the 1500s, the [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Empire) explored the Vietnamese coast and reportedly erected a [stele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stele) on the [Chàm Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%C3%A0m_Islands) to mark their presence.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoang200750-87] By 1533, they began landing in the Vietnamese delta but were forced to leave because of local turmoil and fighting. They also had less interest in the territory than they did in China and Japan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoang200750-87] After they had settled in [Macau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Macau) and [Nagasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecclesiastical_Nagasaki) to begin the profitable Macau–Japan trade route, the Portuguese began to involve themselves in trade with [Hội An](2016839.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoang200750-87] Portuguese traders and [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuits) missionaries under the *[Padroado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padroado)* system were active in both Vietnamese realms of *[Đàng Trong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%A0ng_Trong)* ([Cochinchina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochinchina)) and *[Đàng Ngoài](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%A0ng_Ngo%C3%A0i)* ([Tonkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonkin)) in the 17th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETran2018-88] The [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_colonial_empire) also attempted to establish contact with Cochinchina in 1601 but failed to sustain a presence there after several violent encounters with the locals. The [Dutch East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company) only managed to establish official relations with Tonkin in the spring of 1637 after leaving [Dejima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dejima) in Japan to establish trade for silk.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoang200752-89] Meanwhile, in 1613, the first [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_England) attempt to establish contact with Hội An failed following a violent incident involving the [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company). By 1672, the English established relations with Tonkin and were allowed to reside in [Phố Hiến](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C6%B0ng_Y%C3%AAn_\(city\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoang200753-90]

Between 1615 and 1753, French traders also engaged in trade in Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi199889-91][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELockard2010479-92] The first French missionaries arrived in 1658, under the Portuguese *Padroado*. From its foundation, the [Paris Foreign Missions Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Foreign_Missions_Society) under [*Propaganda Fide*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Evangelization_of_Peoples) actively sent missionaries to Vietnam, entering Cochinchina  in 1664 and Tonkin  in 1666.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETran201727-93] Spanish [Dominicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Order) joined the Tonkin mission in 1676, and [Franciscans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franciscans) were in Cochinchina from 1719 to 1834. Vietnamese authorities became concerned by increasing [Christianisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianization) activities,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcLeod199122-94] and after several Catholic missionaries were detained, the French Navy intervened in 1843 to free them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoods200242-95] In a series of conquests from 1859 to 1885, France eroded Vietnam's sovereignty.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECortada199429-96] At the [siege of Tourane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Tourane) in 1858, France was aided by Spain (with Spanish and Filipino troops from the [Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captaincy_General_of_the_Philippines))[^cite_note-97] and perhaps some Tonkinese Catholics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeith201246-98] After the [1862 Treaty of Saigon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Saigon_\(1862\)), and especially after France completely conquered [Lower Cochinchina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Vietnam) in 1867, the [Văn Thân movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C4%83n_Th%C3%A2n_movement) of scholar-gentry class arose and committed violence against Catholics across central and northern Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeith201249–50-99]

Between 1862 and 1867, the southern third of the country became the [French colony of Cochinchina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Cochinchina).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcLeod199161-100] By 1884, the entire country was under French rule, with the central and northern parts of Vietnam separated into the two protectorates of [Annam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annam_\(French_protectorate\)) and [Tonkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonkin_\(French_protectorate\)). The three entities were formally integrated into the union of [French Indochina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Indochina) in 1887.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOoi2004520-101][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECook2001396-102] During this period, the French administration imposed significant political and cultural changes on Vietnamese society.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrankum2011172-103] A Western-style system of modern education introduced new [humanist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanism) values.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENhu_Nguyen201637-104] Most French settlers in Indochina were concentrated in Cochinchina, particularly in [Saigon](66956.md), and in [Hanoi](56667.md), the colony's capital.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERichardson1876269-105]

During the early colonial period, guerrillas of the royalist [Cần Vương movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%E1%BA%A7n_V%C6%B0%C6%A1ng_movement) rebelled against French rule and massacred around a third of [Vietnam's Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Vietnam) population.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeith201253-106] Anti-Catholic violence persisted in [Nam Định](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nam_%C4%90%E1%BB%8Bnh), [Quảng Trị](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qu%E1%BA%A3ng_Tr%E1%BB%8B_province), and [Bình Định](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%ACnh_%C4%90%E1%BB%8Bnh) during the 1880s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERamsay2008171-107] The French strategy for pacification in Vietnam relied more on alliances with local notables than on Christian missions to establish control and manage resistance.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQuach-Langlet1991361-108] The French developed a [plantation economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plantation_economy) to promote export of tobacco, [indigo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigo_dye), tea and coffee.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELim201433-109] However, they largely ignored the increasing demands for civil rights and [self-governance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-governance). An increasing dissatisfaction even led to half-hearted, badly coordinated, and still worse executed plots to oust the French, such as the [Hanoi Poison Plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanoi_Poison_Plot) of 1908 and the [Thái Nguyên uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%A1i_Nguy%C3%AAn_uprising) of 1917.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZinoman2000-110]



"The Mandarins and the French Authorities Awaiting the Arrival of Emperor [Thành Thái](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%A0nh_Th%C3%A1i)", 1903 oil painting

A nationalist political movement soon emerged, with leaders like [Phan Bội Châu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phan_B%E1%BB%99i_Ch%C3%A2u), [Phan Châu Trinh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phan_Ch%C3%A2u_Trinh), [Phan Đình Phùng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phan_%C4%90%C3%ACnh_Ph%C3%B9ng), and Emperor [Hàm Nghi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%A0m_Nghi) fighting or calling for independence.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELargo2002112-111] This resulted in the 1930 [Yên Bái mutiny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%C3%AAn_B%C3%A1i_mutiny) by the [Vietnamese Nationalist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vi%E1%BB%87t_Nam_Qu%E1%BB%91c_D%C3%A2n_%C4%90%E1%BA%A3ng) (VNQDĐ), which the French quashed. The mutiny split the independence movement, as many leading members converted to [communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhanh_Huynh198698-112][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOdellCastillo200882-113][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas2012-114]

The French maintained full control of their colonies until World War II, when the [war in the Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_War) led to the [Japanese invasion of French Indochina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_invasion_of_French_Indochina) in 1940. Afterwards, the [Japanese Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) was allowed to station its troops in Vietnam while the pro-[Vichy French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vichy_France) colonial administration continued.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiller1990293-115][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGettlemanFranklinYoungFranklin19954-116] Japan exploited Vietnam's natural resources to support its military campaigns, culminating in a [full-scale takeover of the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_coup_d'%C3%A9tat_in_French_Indochina) in March 1945. This led to the [Vietnamese famine of 1944–1945](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_famine_of_1944%E2%80%931945), which killed up to two million people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThanh_Nien2015-117][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_Net''2015-118]

### First Indochina War

Further information: [Civil conflicts in Vietnam (1945–1949)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_conflicts_in_Vietnam_\(1945%E2%80%931949\)), [First Indochina War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Indochina_War), and [1954 Geneva Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Geneva_Conference)

In 1941, the [Việt Minh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viet_Minh), a communist-led national liberation movement, emerged under [Ho Chi Minh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ho_Chi_Minh)'s leadership.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoes199295-119][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPike2011192-120] Mass political mobilization intensified during the [Japanese military occupation of Indochina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Indochina_in_World_War_II),[^cite_note-121] and nationalist parties fostered a stronger sense of national identity.[^cite_note-122] After Japan's defeat in World War II, the Việt Minh seized Hanoi and Huế in August 1945, dissolved the [Empire of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Vietnam), and established a provisional government that declared national independence on 2 September.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPike2011192-120] In the south, Saigon's administrative services collapsed, and chaos, riots, and murder were widespread.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGunn2014270-123]

In August 1945, the [Allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II) had decided to divide Indochina at the [16th parallel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/16th_parallel_north) to allow [Chiang Kai-shek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiang_Kai-shek) of the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\)) to [receive the Japanese surrender in the north](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_occupation_of_northern_Vietnam,_1945%E2%80%931946) while Britain's [Lord Louis Mountbatten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Mountbatten,_1st_Earl_Mountbatten_of_Burma) received their surrender in the south. The Allies agreed that Indochina still belonged to France.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENeville2007175-124][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith20076-125]



First Nationwide Congress of Emulation Fighters, Democratic Republic of Vietnam, 1952&#x20;

But as the French were weakened by the [German occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_military_administration_in_occupied_France_during_World_War_II), [British-Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj) forces and the remaining Japanese [Southern Expeditionary Army Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Expeditionary_Army_Group) were used to maintain order and help France reestablish control through the [1945–1946 War in Vietnam, south of the 16th parallel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_southern_Vietnam_\(1945%E2%80%931946\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENeville2007124-126] The Viet Minh sought to consolidate power by terrorizing and purging rival Vietnamese [nationalist groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_nationalism) and [Trotskyist leaders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trotskyism_in_Vietnam).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuillemot2004-127][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKort201762–63,_81–85-128][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETran202224–30-129][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarr2013383–441-130] In 1946, the Franco-Chinese and [Ho–Sainteny Agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ho%E2%80%93Sainteny_Agreement) enabled French forces to replace the Chinese north of the 16th parallel and facilitated a coexistence between the DRV and the French that strengthened the Viet Minh while undermining the nationalists.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoscha2016204–208-131][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolcombe202035,_38–44-132] That summer, the Viet Minh, in collaboration with French forces, eliminated rival nationalist parties.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKort201783–84-133][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReilly2018175–177-134][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuillemot2004205–207-135][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETran2023699–700-136] Hồ chose to take a compromising stance to avoid military conflict with France, asking the French to withdraw their colonial administrators and for French professors and engineers to help build a modern independent Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPike2011192-120] But [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Government_of_the_French_Republic) did not act on these requests, including the idea of independence, and dispatched the [French Far East Expeditionary Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Far_East_Expeditionary_Corps) to restore colonial rule. With negotiations having broken down,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAsselin202473–81-137] tensions between the Viet Minh and French authorities erupted into the full-scale [First Indochina War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Indochina_War) in December 1946.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoes199295-119][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPike2011192-120][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETonnesson201166-138] Surviving nationalist partisans and politico-religious groups rallied behind the exiled [Bảo Đại](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%E1%BA%A3o_%C4%90%E1%BA%A1i) to reopen negotiations with [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Fourth_Republic), ultimately signing the [Élysée Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lys%C3%A9e_Accords) and establishing the [State of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Vietnam) in opposition to communist domination.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoscha2016238–247-139] The defeat of [French Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Union) forces in the 1954 [Battle of Điện Biên Phủ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_%C4%90i%E1%BB%87n_Bi%C3%AAn_Ph%E1%BB%A7) allowed Hồ to negotiate a ceasefire from a favourable position at the subsequent [Geneva Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Geneva_Conference).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPike2011192-120][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaite201289-140]



Former French Indochina after the [1954 Geneva Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Geneva_Conference), with Vietnam [partitioned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_\(politics\)) along the 17th parallel.

The Geneva Accords of 21 July 1954, signed between the Viet Minh and the French, ended the colonial fighting and affirmed Vietnam's independence, while placing Vietnam under a temporary North–South division along the [Demilitarized Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_Demilitarized_Zone), roughly following the [17th parallel north](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/17th_parallel_north) (pending elections scheduled for July 1956).[^cite_note-145] 300 days of free movement were permitted, during which almost a million northerners, including at least 500,000 Catholics and approximately 200,000 Buddhists, moved south,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoscha2016280-146] fearing persecution by the communists. This migration was aided by the United States navy through [Operation Passage to Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Passage_to_Freedom).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOlson201243-147][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDK201739-148] The [partition of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_Vietnam) by the Geneva Accords was not intended to be permanent, and stipulated that Vietnam would be reunited after the elections.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEvan_DijkGraySavranskayaSuri201368-149] In July 1955, the State of Vietnam's prime minister [Ngô Đình Diệm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngo_Dinh_Diem) announced in a broadcast that South Vietnam would not participate in the elections, as they had not signed the accords and therefore were not bound by them.[^cite_note-cđcn-150][^cite_note-Cheng1997p11-151] In October 1955, Ngô Đình Diệm toppled [Bảo Đại](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%E1%BA%A3o_%C4%90%E1%BA%A1i) in a fraudulent [referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1955_State_of_Vietnam_referendum) organised by his brother [Ngô Đình Nhu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ng%C3%B4_%C4%90%C3%ACnh_Nhu), and proclaimed himself president of the [Republic of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Vietnam).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEvan_DijkGraySavranskayaSuri201368-149]

### Vietnam War

Main article: [Vietnam War](32611.md)

From 1953 to 1956, the [North Vietnamese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Vietnam) government instituted [agrarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agrarianism) reforms including "[rent reduction](41258426.md)" and "[land reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reform_in_North_Vietnam)", which resulted in significant [political repression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_repression).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoise201756-152] This included 13,500 to as many as 100,000 executions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVu2007-153][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETurner1975143-154] In the South, Diệm countered North Vietnamese subversion (including the assassination of over 450 South Vietnamese officials in 1956) by detaining tens of thousands of suspected communists in "political reeducation centres".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeneghan1969160-155][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETurner1975177-156] This programme incarcerated many non-communists, but was successful at curtailing [communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism) activity in the country, if only for a time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECrozier1955-157] The North Vietnamese government claimed that 2,148 people were killed in the process by November 1957.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETurner1975174–178-158] The pro-Hanoi [Viet Cong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viet_Cong) began a guerrilla campaign in [South Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Vietnam) in the late 1950s to overthrow Diệm's government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGilbert2013292-159] From 1960, the Soviet Union and North Vietnam signed treaties providing for further Soviet military support.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJukes1973209-160][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOlsen200792-161][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhoo201127-162] The Republic of Vietnam in the south was supported by the [United States](3434750.md), [Australia](4689264.md), [South Korea](27019.md), and [Thailand](30128.md), while the Democratic Republic of Vietnam in the north was supported by the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union), [People's Republic of China](5405.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEvan_DijkGraySavranskayaSuri201368-149] [Khmer Rouge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmer_Rouge), and later, [Sweden](5058739.md).[^cite_note-163]

Three US Fairchild UC-123B aircraft pictured spraying Agent Orange

&#x20;Three US [Fairchild UC-123B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairchild_C-123_Provider) aircraft spraying [Agent Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agent_Orange) during the [Operation Ranch Hand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Ranch_Hand) as part of a [herbicidal warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbicidal_warfare) operation depriving the food and vegetation cover of the [Việt Cộng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vi%E1%BB%87t_C%E1%BB%99ng), c. 1962–1971

In 1963, Buddhist discontent with Diệm's perceived pro-Catholic bias erupted into [mass demonstrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_crisis), leading to a violent government crackdown.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMuehlenbeckMuehlenbeck2012221-164] This led to the [collapse of Diệm's relationship with the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cable_243), and ultimately to a [1963 coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1963_South_Vietnamese_coup) in which [he and Nhu were assassinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_and_assassination_of_Ng%C3%B4_%C4%90%C3%ACnh_Di%E1%BB%87m).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWillbanks201353-165] The Diệm era was followed by more than a dozen successive military governments, before the pairing of Air Marshal [Nguyễn Cao Kỳ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_Cao_K%E1%BB%B3) and General [Nguyễn Văn Thiệu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_V%C4%83n_Thi%E1%BB%87u) took control in mid-1965.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuy_HinhDinh_Tho2015238-166] Thiệu gradually outmaneuvered Kỳ and cemented his grip on power in fraudulent elections in 1967 and 1971.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIssermanBowman200946-167] During this political instability, the communists began to gain ground. To support South Vietnam's struggle against the communist insurgency, the United States used the 1964 [Gulf of Tonkin incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Tonkin_incident) as a pretext for increasing its contribution of military advisers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlterman2005213-168] US forces became involved in ground combat operations by 1965, and at their peak several years later, numbered more than 500,000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELewy1980-169][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibbons2014166-170] The US also engaged in [sustained aerial bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Rolling_Thunder). Meanwhile, China and the Soviet Union provided North Vietnam with significant material aid and 15,000 combat advisers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJukes1973209-160][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOlsen200792-161][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi201267-171] Communist forces supplying the Việt Cộng carried supplies along the [Ho Chi Minh trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ho_Chi_Minh_trail), which passed through [Laos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Laos).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGillet2011-172]

The communists attacked South Vietnamese targets during the 1968 [Tết Offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%E1%BA%BFt_Offensive). The campaign failed militarily, but shocked the American establishment and turned US public opinion against the war.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDallek2018-173] During the offensive, communist troops [massacred over 3,000 civilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massacre_at_Hu%E1%BA%BF) at [Huế](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hu%E1%BA%BF).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETurner1975251-174][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrankum2011209-175] Facing an increasing casualty count, [rising domestic opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition_to_United_States_involvement_in_the_Vietnam_War) to the war, and growing international condemnation, the US began [withdrawing from ground combat roles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nixon_Doctrine) in the early 1970s. This also entailed an unsuccessful effort to [strengthen and stabilise South Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamisation).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEggleston20141-176] Following the [Paris Peace Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Peace_Accords) of 27 January 1973, all American combat troops were withdrawn by 29 March 1973.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory2018-177] In December 1974, North Vietnam [captured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Ph%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Bc_Long) the province of [Phước Long](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ph%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Bc_Long_province) and started a [full-scale offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1975_Spring_Offensive), culminating in the [fall of Saigon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Saigon) on 30 April 1975.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETucker2011749-178] South Vietnam was ruled by a [provisional government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Revolutionary_Government_of_the_Republic_of_South_Vietnam) for 14 months under North Vietnamese control.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrigham199886-179]

### Reunification and reforms

Further information: [Re-education camp (Vietnam)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re-education_camp_\(Vietnam\)), [Vietnamese boat people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_boat_people), and [Đổi Mới](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%E1%BB%95i_M%E1%BB%9Bi)

On 2 July 1976, North and South Vietnam were merged to form the Socialist Republic of Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_New_York_Times''1976-180] The war had devastated Vietnam and killed 966,000 to 3.8 million people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHirschmanPrestonManh_Loi1995-181][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShenon1995-182][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEObermeyerMurrayGakidou2008-183] A 1974 US Senate subcommittee estimated nearly 1.4 million [Vietnamese civilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_War_casualties) were killed or wounded between 1965 and 1974—including 415,000 killed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDohrenwendTurseWallYager201869-184][^cite_note-185] In its aftermath, under [Lê Duẩn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%AA_Du%E1%BA%A9n)'s administration, there were no mass executions of South Vietnamese who had served the defunct South Vietnamese government, confounding Western fears,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEElliott2010499,_512–513-186] but up to 300,000 South Vietnamese were sent to [reeducation camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re-education_camp_\(Vietnam\)), where many endured torture, starvation, and disease while being forced to perform hard labour.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESaganDenny1982-187] The government embarked on a mass campaign of [collectivisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivisation) of farms and factories.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpokesman-Review19778-188] [Millions fled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indochina_refugee_crisis) the country in the aftermath of the war and during the [subsidy period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsidy_period).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoise198812-189] In 1978, in response to the [Khmer Rouge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmer_Rouge) government of Cambodia ordering massacres of Vietnamese residents in the border villages in the districts of [An Giang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An_Giang_province) and [Kiên Giang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ki%C3%AAn_Giang_province),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKissi2006144-190] the Vietnamese military [invaded Cambodia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambodian%E2%80%93Vietnamese_War) and removed them from power after occupying [Phnom Penh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phnom_Penh).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeggle2004166-191] The intervention was a success, resulting in the establishment of a new, pro-Vietnam socialist government, the [People's Republic of Kampuchea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Republic_of_Kampuchea), which ruled until 1989.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHampson1996175-192] However, this worsened relations with China, which had supported the Khmer Rouge. China later launched a [brief incursion into northern Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-Vietnamese_War) in 1979, causing Vietnam to rely even more heavily on Soviet economic and military aid, while mistrust of the [Chinese government](23239.md) escalated.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhoo2011131-193]

At the [Sixth National Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6th_National_Congress_of_the_Communist_Party_of_Vietnam) of the [Communist Party of Vietnam](400546.md) (CPV) in December 1986, reformist politicians replaced the "old guard" government with new leadership.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News1997-194][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Phuc2014-195] The reformers were led by 71-year-old [Nguyễn Văn Linh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_V%C4%83n_Linh), who became the party's new general secretary.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News1997-194] He and the reformers implemented a series of market reforms known as *[Đổi Mới](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%E1%BB%95i_M%E1%BB%9Bi)* ("Renovation") that carefully managed the transition from a [planned economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned_economy) to a "[socialist-oriented market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist-oriented_market_economy)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMurray199724–25-196][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBich_Loan2007-197] Although the authority of the state remained unchallenged under *Đổi Mới*, the government encouraged [private ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_ownership) of farms and factories, economic deregulation, and foreign investment, while maintaining control over strategic industries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBich_Loan2007-197][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHowe201620-198] While Vietnam's reforms catalysed rapid industrial and export growth, they also widened income and gender disparities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoodkind1995-199][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGallup2002-200][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWagstaffvan_DoorslaerWatanabe2003-201]

In 2021, [General Secretary of the Communist Party of Vietnam](7482670.md), [Nguyễn Phú Trọng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_Ph%C3%BA_Tr%E1%BB%8Dng), was re-elected for his third term in office, meaning he was Vietnam's most powerful leader in decades.[^cite_note-202] He died 19 July 2024, and was followed by [Tô Lâm](54878346.md) as General Secretary of the Communist Party.[^cite_note-203]

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Vietnam)

Images showing Hạ Long Bay, the Yên River and the Bản-Giốc Waterfalls

Natural attractions in Vietnam, clockwise from top: [Hạ Long Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%E1%BA%A1_Long_Bay), [Yên River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%C3%AAn_River), and [Bản-Giốc Waterfalls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Gioc%E2%80%93Detian_Falls)

Image of the Hoàng Liên Sơn mountain range

Hoàng Liên Sơn mountain range, the range that includes [Fansipan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fansipan), which is the highest summit on the Indochinese Peninsula

Vietnam is located on the eastern [Indochinese Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainland_Southeast_Asia) between the latitudes [8°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8th_parallel_north) and [24°N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/24th_parallel_north), and the longitudes [102°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/102nd_meridian_east) and [110°E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/110th_meridian_east). It covers a total area of 331,210 km<sup>2</sup> (127,881 sq mi)[^cite_note-Vietnam-1] or 331,699 km<sup>2</sup> (128,070 sq mi).[^cite_note-Vietnam_country_profile-6] The combined length of the country's land boundaries is 4,639 km (2,883 mi), and its coastline is 3,444 km (2,140 mi) long.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENasuchon20087-204] At its narrowest point in the central [Quảng Bình province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qu%E1%BA%A3ng_B%C3%ACnh_province), the country is as little as 50 kilometres (31 mi) across, though it widens to around 600 kilometres (370 mi) in the north.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEProtected_Areas_and_Development_Partnership200313-205] Vietnam's land is mostly hilly and densely forested, with level land covering no more than 20%. Mountains account for 40% of the country's land area,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrohlichSchreinemachersStahrClemens20135-206] and [tropical forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_forest) cover around 42%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENatural_Resources_and_Environment_Program199556-207] The [Red River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_River_\(Asia\)) flows past Hanoi in Northern Vietnam before emptying into the [Gulf of Tonkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Tonkin). The [Red River Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_River_Delta) is a flat, roughly triangular low-level plain covering 15,000 km<sup>2</sup> (5,792 sq mi).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''AgroViet_Newsletter''2007-208] It has the smallest area but the highest population and population density of all [regions in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regions_of_Vietnam). It is criss-crossed by a maze of rivers and canals, which carry so much [sediment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alluvium) that the delta advances 60 to 80 metres (196.9 to 262.5 ft) into the South China Sea every year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuu_Chiem1993183-209][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHong_TruongYeStive2017757-210] The [exclusive economic zone of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone_of_Vietnam) covers 417,663 km<sup>2</sup> (161,261 sq mi) in the [South China Sea](74209.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVietnamese_Waters_Zone-211]

Southern Vietnam is divided into coastal lowlands, the mountains of the [Annamite Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annamite_Range), and extensive forests. Comprising five relatively flat plateaus of [basalt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basalt) soil, the highlands account for 16% of the country's [arable land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arable_land) and 22% of its total forested land.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECosslettCosslett201713-212] The soil in much of the southern part of Vietnam is relatively low in nutrients as a result of intense cultivation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_DeDouglasMcMorrowLindley2008-213] Several minor earthquakes have been recorded.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHong_Phuong20123-214][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Viet_Nam_News''2016-215] The northern part of the country consists mostly of highlands and the Red River Delta. [Fansipan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fansipan) ([Vietnamese](32511.md): *Phan Xi Păng*), the summit of which is located on the border between [Lào Cai](27784276.md) and [Lai Châu](39952604.md) provinces, is the highest mountain on the Indochinese Peninsula, standing 3,143 m (10,312 ft) high.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_National_Administration_of_Tourism''2014-216] From north to south Vietnam, the country also has [numerous islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_islands_of_Vietnam); [Phú Quốc](1677899.md) is the largest.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoobbyerSpooner2013173-217] [Ba Bể Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ba_B%E1%BB%83_Lake) is the largest natural lake in Vietnam, and the [Mekong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mekong) River is the longest river in the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECosslettCosslett201313-218][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnh2016a-219][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_Telegraph''-220] [Hang Sơn Đoòng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hang_S%C6%A1n_%C4%90o%C3%B2ng), first surveyed in 2009 by an expedition from the [British Cave Research Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Cave_Research_Association), is among the largest caves in the world.[^cite_note-Owen2009-221] The cave is over 8.8 km (5.5 mi) in length, and some of its caverns are large enough to hold a 40-story skyscraper. It has an enormous [sinkhole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinkhole), more than 183 m (600 ft) in depth, beneath which grows a tropical rainforest containing trees nearly 30 m (98 ft) tall. This forest is home to monkeys and other animals not normally found in a subterranean environment. A [subterranean river](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subterranean_river) also flows within the cave.[^cite_note-Geiling2014-222]

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Vietnam)

&#x20;An image of the Köppen climate classification map of Vietnam

[Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) map of Vietnam

Photograph of Nha Trang beach with many high rise buildings behind it

[Nha Trang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nha_Trang), a popular beach destination, has a [tropical savanna climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_savanna_climate)

Due to differences in latitude and the marked variety in [topographical relief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topographical_relief), Vietnam's climate tends to vary considerably for each region.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVu197966-223] During the winter or dry season, extending roughly from November to April, the [monsoon](57630.md) winds usually blow from the northeast along the Chinese coast and across the Gulf of Tonkin, picking up considerable moisture.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERiehlAugstein19731-224] The average annual temperature is generally higher in the plains than in the mountains, especially in southern Vietnam compared to the north. The northern region experiences a monsoonal and humid subtropical climate with four distinct seasons (spring, summer, autumn, and winter), with winters typically dry and summers ranging from hot to mild.[^cite_note-225] In southern and central areas, the climate is tropical monsoon with only two seasons (rainy and dry).[^cite_note-vietnamkhihau-226][^cite_note-vietnamiaea2012-227][^cite_note-VNATclimate-228] Temperatures vary less in the southern plains around Ho Chi Minh City and the Mekong Delta, ranging from between 21 and 35 °C (70 and 95 °F) over the year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuleen2017-229] In Hanoi and the surrounding areas of the Red River Delta, the temperatures are much lower between 15 and 33 °C (59 and 91 °F).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuleen2017-229] Seasonal variations in the mountains, plateaus, and the northernmost areas are much more dramatic, with temperatures varying from 3 °C (37 °F) in December and January to 37 °C (99 °F) in July and August.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVietnam_Net2018a-230] During winter, snow occasionally falls over the highest peaks of the far northern mountains near the Chinese border.[^cite_note-231] Vietnam receives high rates of [precipitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precipitation) in the form of rainfall with an average amount from 1,500 to 2,000 mm (60 to 80 in) during the monsoon seasons; this often causes flooding, especially in the cities with poor drainage systems.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThi_Anh-232] The country is also affected by [tropical depressions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_depression), [tropical storms](8282374.md) and [typhoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoon).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThi_Anh-232] Vietnam is one of the most vulnerable countries to climate change, with 55% of its population living in low-elevation coastal areas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOverland2017-233][^cite_note-234]

### Biodiversity

Main articles: [Wildlife of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Vietnam), [Environmental issues in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_Vietnam), [List of endangered species in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_endangered_species_in_Vietnam), and [Protected areas of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Vietnam)

Photographs of Native species in Vietnam, the crested argus; the red-shanked douc, a monkey; the Indochinese leopard and the saola, a bovine

Native species in Vietnam, clockwise from top-right: [crested argus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crested_argus), [Tonkin snub-nosed monkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonkin_snub-nosed_monkey), [Indochinese leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indochinese_leopard), and [saola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saola)

Photograph of the Sa Pa mountain hills with agricultural activity shiwn in the foreground

&#x20;[Sa Pa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sa_Pa_\(town\)) mountain hills with [agricultural activities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Vietnam)

As the country is located within the [Indomalayan realm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indomalayan_realm), Vietnam is one of twenty-five countries considered to possess a uniquely high level of [biodiversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity). This was noted in the country's National Environmental Condition Report in 2005.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Natural_Resources_and_Environment''-235] It is ranked 16th worldwide in biological diversity, being home to approximately 16% of the world's species. 15,986 species of [flora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flora) have been identified in the country, of which 10% are [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemism). Vietnam's [fauna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fauna) includes 307 [nematode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nematode) species, 200 [oligochaeta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oligochaeta), 145 [acarina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acarina), 113 [springtails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springtail), 7,750 insects, 260 reptiles, and 120 amphibians. There are 840 birds and 310 mammals found in Vietnam, of which 100 birds and 78 mammals are endemic.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Natural_Resources_and_Environment''-235] Vietnam has two [World Natural Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Natural_Heritage_Site)—the [Hạ Long Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%E1%BA%A1_Long_Bay) and [Phong Nha-Kẻ Bàng National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phong_Nha-K%E1%BA%BB_B%C3%A0ng_National_Park)—together with nine [biosphere reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Network_of_Biosphere_Reserves_in_Asia_and_the_Pacific#Vietnam), including [Cần Giờ Mangrove Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%E1%BA%A7n_Gi%E1%BB%9D_Mangrove_Forest), [Cát Tiên](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%A1t_Ti%C3%AAn_National_Park), [Cát Bà](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%A1t_B%C3%A0_National_Park), [Kiên Giang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U_Minh_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%A3ng_National_Park), the Red River Delta, Mekong Delta, [Western Nghệ An](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Ngh%E1%BB%87_An), [Cà Mau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C5%A9i_C%C3%A0_Mau_National_Park), and [Cu Lao Cham Marine Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cu_Lao_Cham_Marine_Park).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNESCO_World_Heritage_Convention1994-236][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNESCO_World_Heritage_Convention2003-237]

Vietnam is also home to 1,438 species of freshwater [microalgae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microalgae), constituting 9.6% of all microalgae species, as well as 794 aquatic [invertebrates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invertebrate) and 2,458 species of sea fish.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Natural_Resources_and_Environment''-235] In recent years, 13 [genera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genera), 222 species, and 30 [taxa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxa) of flora have been newly described in Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Natural_Resources_and_Environment''-235] Six new mammal species, including the [saola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saola), [giant muntjac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giant_muntjac), and [Tonkin snub-nosed monkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonkin_snub-nosed_monkey) have also been discovered, along with one new bird species, the endangered [Edwards's pheasant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edwards's_pheasant).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBirdLife_International2016-238] In the late 1980s, a small population of [Javan rhinoceros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javan_rhinoceros) was found in Cát Tiên National Park. However, the last individual of the species in Vietnam was reportedly shot in 2010.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKinver2011-239] In agricultural [genetic diversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetic_diversity), Vietnam is one of the world's twelve original [cultivar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultivar) centres. The Vietnam National Cultivar Gene Bank preserves 12,300 cultivars of 115 species.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Natural_Resources_and_Environment''-235] The Vietnamese government spent US\$49.07 million on the preservation of biodiversity in 2004 alone and has established 126 conservation areas, including 30 [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_Vietnam).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Natural_Resources_and_Environment''-235]

In Vietnam, wildlife [poaching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poaching) has become a major concern. In 2000, a [non-governmental organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-governmental_organisation) (NGO), Education for Nature – Vietnam, was founded to instill in the population the importance of wildlife conservation in the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDall2017-240] In the years that followed, another NGO called GreenViet was formed by Vietnamese youngsters for the enforcement of wildlife protection. Through collaboration between the NGOs and local authorities, many local poaching syndicates were crippled by their leaders' arrests.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDall2017-240] A study released in 2018 revealed Vietnam is a destination for the illegal export of [rhinoceros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhinoceros) horns from [South Africa](17416221.md) due to the demand for them as a medicine and a status symbol.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDang_VuNielsen2018-241][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENam_DangNielsen2019-242]

The main environmental concern that persists in Vietnam today is the legacy of the use of the chemical [herbicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbicide) [Agent Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agent_Orange), which continues to cause [birth defects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_defect) and many health problems in the Vietnamese population. In the southern and central areas affected most by the chemical's use during the Vietnam War, nearly 4.8 million Vietnamese people have been exposed to it and suffered from its effects.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBanoutUrbanMusilSzakova2014-243][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECerre2016-244][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown2018-245] In 2012, approximately 50 years after the war,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAFP_staff2016-246] the US began a US\$43 million joint clean-up project in the former chemical storage areas in Vietnam to take place in stages.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECerre2016-244][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMacLeod2012-247] Following the completion of the first phase in [Đà Nẵng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%A0_N%E1%BA%B5ng) in late 2017,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''United_States_Agency_for_International_Development''-248] the US announced its commitment to clean other sites, especially in the heavily impacted site of [Biên Hòa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bi%C3%AAn_H%C3%B2a).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStewart2018-249]

The Vietnamese government spends over [VNĐ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_%C4%91%E1%BB%93ng)10 trillion each year (\$431.1 million) for monthly allowances and the physical rehabilitation of victims of the chemicals.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Viet_Nam_News''2018a-250] In 2018, the Japanese engineering group [Shimizu Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimizu_Corporation), working with the Vietnamese military, built a plant for the treatment of soil polluted by Agent Orange. Plant construction costs were funded by the company itself.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Nikkei_Asian_Review''2018-251][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENHK_World-Japan2018-252] One of the long-term plans to restore southern Vietnam's damaged [ecosystems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecosystem) is through the use of [reforestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reforestation) efforts. The Vietnamese government began doing this at the end of the war. It started by replanting [mangrove forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangrove_forest) in the Mekong Delta regions and in [Cần Giờ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%E1%BA%A7n_Gi%E1%BB%9D_district) outside Ho Chi Minh City, where mangroves are important to ease (though not eliminate) flood conditions during monsoon seasons.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgent_Orange_Record-253] The country had a 2019 [Forest Landscape Integrity Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index) mean score of 5.35/10, ranking it 104th globally out of 172 countries.[^cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-254]

Apart from herbicide problems, [arsenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arsenic_poisoning) in the [ground water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground_water) in the Mekong and Red River Deltas has also become a major concern.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBergStengelPhamPham2007-255][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMerolaHienQuyenVengosh2014-256] And most notoriously, [unexploded ordnances](406804.md) (UXO) pose dangers to humans and wildlife—another bitter legacy from the long wars.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiguelRoland2005-257] As part of the continuous campaign to [demine](2206580.md)/remove UXOs, several international [bomb removal agencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mine_clearance_agency) from the United Kingdom,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_the_United_Kingdom''2017-258] Denmark,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELM_Report2000-259] [South Korea](27019.md)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_Development_Programme2018-260] and the US[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''United_States_Department_of_State''2006-261] have been providing assistance. The Vietnamese government spends over VNĐ1 trillion (\$44 million) annually on demining operations and additional hundreds of billions of đồng for treatment, assistance, rehabilitation, vocational training, and resettlement of the victims of UXOs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Thanh2016-262]



Panoramic view of [Hạ Long Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%E1%BA%A1_Long_Bay)

## Government and politics

Main articles: [Politics of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Vietnam) and [Government of Vietnam](78640.md)

Tô Lâm

[Tô Lâm](54878346.md)\
[General Secretary](7482670.md) and [President](466686.md)

Lê Minh Hưng

[Lê Minh Hưng](56144048.md)\
[Prime Minister](439887.md)

Trần Thanh Mẫn

[Trần Thanh Mẫn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tr%E1%BA%A7n_Thanh_M%E1%BA%ABn)\
[National Assembly Chairman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairperson_of_the_National_Assembly_of_Vietnam)

Vietnam is a [unitary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) [communist state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_state) that self-designates as a [socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_state_\(communism\)) [republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic), one of the two communist states (the other being [Laos](17752.md)) in [Southeast Asia](28741.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_Vietnam_(II)''-263] Although Vietnam remains officially committed to [socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism) as its defining creed, some external observers have stated its economic policies have grown increasingly [capitalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitalism),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreenfield1994204-264][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBacciniImpullittiMalesky2017-265] with *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* characterising its leadership as "ardently capitalist communists".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_Economist''2008-266] Under the [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Vietnam), the [Communist Party of Vietnam](400546.md) (CPV) asserts their role in all sectors of the country's politics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_Vietnam_(II)''-263] The [president](466686.md) is the elected [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state) and the [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) of the military, serving as the chairman of the Council of Supreme Defence and Security, and holds the second highest office in Vietnam as well as performing executive functions and state appointments and setting policy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_Vietnam_(II)''-263]

The [general secretary](7482670.md) of the CPV performs numerous key administrative functions, controlling the party's national organisation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_Vietnam_(II)''-263] The [prime minister](439887.md) is the [head of government](37570.md), presiding over a council of ministers composed of five deputy prime ministers and the heads of 26 ministries and commissions. Only political organisations affiliated with or endorsed by the CPV are permitted to contest elections in Vietnam. These include the [Vietnamese Fatherland Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_Fatherland_Front) and worker and [trade unionist](17626.md) parties.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_Vietnam_(II)''-263]

Photograph of the National Assembly of Vietnam in Hanoi

&#x20;The [National Assembly of Vietnam](4420212.md) building in Hanoi

The [National Assembly of Vietnam](4420212.md) is the [unicameral](204304.md) [supreme state organ of power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_state_organ_of_power) composed of 500 members.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEmbassy_of_Vietnam_in_USA-267] Headed by a [chairman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Chairmen_of_the_Standing_Committee_of_the_National_Assembly_of_Vietnam), it holds the [unified powers of the state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unified_power) (meaning that it is superior to both the executive and judicial organs), with all central state officials being appointed from members of the National Assembly.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_Vietnam_(II)''-263] The [Supreme People's Court of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_People's_Court_of_Vietnam), headed by a chief justice, is the country's highest [court of appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_appeal), though it is also answerable to the National Assembly. Beneath the Supreme People's Court stand the [provincial municipal courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_Municipal_Courts_of_Vietnam) and many [local courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_Courts_of_Vietnam). [Military courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Courts_of_Vietnam) possess special jurisdiction in matters of [state security](240468.md). Vietnam maintains the [death penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_Vietnam) for numerous offences.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Justice''1999-268]

In 2023, a three-person collective leadership was responsible for governing Vietnam. President [Võ Văn Thưởng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%B5_V%C4%83n_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng),[^cite_note-269] Prime Minister [Phạm Minh Chính](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ph%E1%BA%A1m_Minh_Ch%C3%ADnh) (since 2021)[^cite_note-270] and the most powerful leader [Nguyễn Phú Trọng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_Ph%C3%BA_Tr%E1%BB%8Dng) (since 2011) as the Communist Party of Vietnam's General Secretary.[^cite_note-271] On 22 May 2024, [Tô Lâm](54878346.md), who previously served as the [Minister of Public Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Public_Security_\(Vietnam\)), was voted as the president of Vietnam by the National Assembly, after Võ Văn Thưởng resigned in March of the same year due to corruption charges against him.[^cite_note-272] On 3 August 2024, Tô Lâm, who is also serving as the president, was elected by the Central Committee of the Communist Party of Vietnam as the general secretary following the death of Nguyễn Phú Trọng on 19 July 2024.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] On 21 October 2024, the National Assembly appointed [army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Army_of_Vietnam) general [Lương Cường](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C6%B0%C6%A1ng_C%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Dng) as president, succeeding Tô Lâm.[^cite_note-275]

### Administrative divisions

Main articles: [Subdivisions of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdivisions_of_Vietnam) and [Provinces of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Vietnam)
