Following the [2025 administrative reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Vietnamese_administrative_reforms) and subsequent administrative changes, Vietnam is divided into 25 [provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Vietnam) (Vietnamese: *tỉnh*) and 9 [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Vietnam)[^cite_note-276] (Vietnamese: *thành phố trực thuộc trung ương*), which are administratively on the same level as provinces.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280]

Both provinces and centrally-controlled municipalities are subdivided into [wards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ward_\(Vietnam\)) (*phường*), [communes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commune_\(Vietnam\)) (*xã*) and [special zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_zone_\(Vietnam\)) (*đặc khu*).

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Vietnam)

Nguyễn Phú Trọng and Xi Jinping

General Secretary [Nguyễn Phú Trọng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_Ph%C3%BA_Tr%E1%BB%8Dng) with Chinese President [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) on 13 December 2023

Nguyễn Phú Trọng and Vladimir Putin

General Secretary Nguyễn Phú Trọng with Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) on 20 June 2024

Tô Lâm and Joe Biden

General Secretary [Tô Lâm](54878346.md) with US President [Joe Biden](145422.md) on 25 September 2024

Throughout its history, Vietnam's main foreign relationship has been with various Chinese dynasties.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThayer1994-281] Its principles of sovereignty and insistence on cultural independence are reflected in historical documents such as the 11th-century poem *[Nam quốc sơn hà](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nam_qu%E1%BB%91c_s%C6%A1n_h%C3%A0)* and the 1428 proclamation *[Bình Ngô đại cáo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%ACnh_Ng%C3%B4_%C4%91%E1%BA%A1i_c%C3%A1o)*. Following the [partition of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_Vietnam) in 1954, [North Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Vietnam) maintained relations with the [Eastern Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Bloc), while [South Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Vietnam) maintained relations with the [Western Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Bloc).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThayer1994-281] Vietnam holds membership in 63 international organisations, including the [United Nations](31769.md) (UN), [Association of Southeast Asian Nations](106539.md) (ASEAN), [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement) (NAM), [International Organisation of the Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie) (La Francophonie), and [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) (WTO). It also maintains relations with over 650 non-governmental organisations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs''2018-282] As of 2010, Vietnam had established diplomatic relations with 178 countries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs''2013-283]

Vietnam's current foreign policy is to consistently implement a policy of independence, self-reliance, peace, co-operation, and development, as well as openness, diversification, [multilateralisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multilateralism) with international relations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs''2007-284][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs''2014-285] The country declares itself a friend and partner of all countries in the international community, regardless of their political affiliation, by actively taking part in international and regional cooperative development projects.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBich_Loan2007-197][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs''2007-284] Since the 1990s, Vietnam has taken several key steps to restore diplomatic ties with [Western countries](21208200.md). It already had relations with some of them in the decades prior.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDayley201898-286] Relations with the United States [began improving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States%E2%80%93Vietnam_relations#Rapprochement) in August 1995 with both states upgrading their *[liaison](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/liaison)* offices to embassy status.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMitchell1995-287] In May 2016, US President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) further normalised relations with Vietnam by announcing the lifting of the arms [embargo](2030237.md) on lethal weapons sales.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGaramone2016-288] Vietnam nowadays has pursued closer ties with the US, especially in the geopolitical context of its [territorial disputes with China in the South China Sea](32005536.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHutt2020-289][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETran2020-290] Though China and Vietnam are now formally at peace,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThayer1994-281] significant tensions remain between the two countries over the these issues.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThanh_Hai2016177-291] The [two countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Vietnam_relations), however, maintain extensive economic and political ties.[^cite_note-\:13-292]<sup>: 102 </sup> Vietnam's foreign policy is now focused on balancing its ties with both China and the United States.[^cite_note-293] Vietnam also maintains close military and security [relations with Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia%E2%80%93Vietnam_relations).[^cite_note-294]

### Military

Main article: [Vietnam People's Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_People's_Armed_Forces)

The [Vietnam People's Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_People's_Armed_Forces) consists of the [Vietnam People's Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_People's_Army) (VPA), the [Vietnam People's Public Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_People's_Public_Security), and the Vietnam Self-Defence Militia. The VPA is the official name for the active military services of Vietnam, and is subdivided into the [Vietnam People's Ground Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_People's_Ground_Forces), the [Vietnam People's Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_People's_Navy), the [Vietnam People's Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_People's_Air_Force), the [Vietnam Border Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_Border_Guard), and the [Vietnam Coast Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_Coast_Guard). The VPA has an active manpower of around 450,000, but its total strength, including paramilitary forces, may be as high as 5,000,000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylorRutherford201150-295] In 2015, Vietnam's [military expenditure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_military_expenditures) totalled approximately US\$4.4 billion, equivalent to around 8% of its total government spending.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYan2016-296] Joint military exercises and war games have been held with [Brunei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brunei),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVoice_of_Vietnam2016-297] China,[^cite_note-298] India,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_Economic_Times''2018-299] Japan,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_Japan_Times''2015-300] Laos,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVoice_of_Vietnam2018b-301] Russia,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Defence''_Russia2018-302] [Singapore](27318.md)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVoice_of_Vietnam2016-297] and the US.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_Telegraph''2012-303]

### Human rights and sociopolitical issues

See also: [Human rights in Vietnam](4321666.md)

Vietnam's pre-1986 communist system has been described either as [totalitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Totalitarianism)[^cite_note-tot2-304] or not totalitarian but autocratic.[^cite_note-305] Since 1986, Vietnam retreated from totalitarianism to authoritarianism.[^cite_note-tot1-306][^cite_note-tot2-304] Under the current constitution, the CPV is the only party allowed to rule, the operation of all other political parties being outlawed. Other human rights issues concern [freedom of association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_association), [freedom of speech](21401843.md), [freedom of religion](46006.md), and [freedom of the press](105323.md). In 2009, Vietnamese lawyer [Lê Công Định](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%AA_C%C3%B4ng_%C4%90%E1%BB%8Bnh) was arrested and charged with the capital crime of [subversion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subversion); several of his associates were also arrested.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News2009-307][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMydans2009-308] [Amnesty International](18947898.md) described him and his arrested associates as [prisoners of conscience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prisoners_of_conscience).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News2009-307] Vietnam has also suffered from human trafficking and related issues.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310][^cite_note-auto3-311]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Vietnam)



Historical GDP per capita development of Vietnam

| Share of world [GDP](12594.md) ([PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity))[^cite_note-IMFWEO.VN-9] |  |
| --- | --- |
| Year | Share |
| 1980 | 0.21% |
| 1990 | 0.28% |
| 2000 | 0.39% |
| 2010 | 0.52% |
| 2020 | 0.80% |

Throughout the history of Vietnam, its economy has been based largely on [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture)—primarily [wet rice cultivation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice_production_in_Vietnam).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECornell_University-312] [Bauxite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bauxite_mining_in_Vietnam), an important material in the production of [aluminium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aluminium), is mined in central Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKim_Phuong20141-313] Since reunification, the country's economy is shaped primarily by the CPV through [Five-Year Plans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five-Year_Plans_of_Vietnam) decided upon at the plenary sessions of the Central Committee and national congresses.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKimura1986-314] The [collectivisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivisation) of farms, factories, and capital goods was carried out as part of the establishment of central planning, with millions of people working for state enterprises. Under strict state control, Vietnam's economy continued to be plagued by inefficiency, [corruption in state-owned enterprises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_in_Vietnam), poor quality, and underproduction.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdhikariKirkpatrickWeiss1992249-315][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Tho200311-316] With the decline in economic aid from its main trading partner, the Soviet Union, following the erosion of the [Eastern bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_bloc) in the late 1980s, and the subsequent [collapse of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union), as well as the negative impacts of the post-war [trade embargo](411315.md) imposed by the United States,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELitvackLitvackRondinelli199931-317][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreeman2002-318] Vietnam began to liberalise its trade by [devaluing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devaluation) its exchange rate to increase exports and embarked on a policy of economic development.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELitvackLitvackRondinelli199933-319]

Photograph of Vietnam's tallest skyscraper, the Landmark 81, located in Bình Thạnh District in Ho Chi Minh City

Vietnam's tallest skyscraper, the [Landmark 81](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landmark_81), located in [Bình Thạnh district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%ACnh_Th%E1%BA%A1nh_district), [Ho Chi Minh City](66956.md).

In 1986, the [Sixth National Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6th_National_Congress_of_the_Communist_Party_of_Vietnam) of the CPV introduced [socialist-oriented market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist-oriented_market_economy) economic reforms as part of the *[Đổi Mới](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%E1%BB%95i_M%E1%BB%9Bi)* reform programme. [Private ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_ownership) began to be encouraged in industry, commerce, and agriculture, and state enterprises were [restructured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporatisation) to operate under market constraints.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Tho20035-320][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoang_VuongDung_Tran2009-321] This led to the five-year economic plans being replaced by the socialist-oriented market mechanism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoang_Vuong2014-322] As a result of these reforms, Vietnam achieved approximately 8% annual [gross domestic product](12594.md) (GDP) growth between 1990 and 1997.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELargo200266-323][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternational_Monetary_Fund199923-324] The United States ended its economic embargo against Vietnam in early 1994.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECockburn1994-325] Although the [1997 Asian financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Asian_financial_crisis) caused an economic slowdown to 4–5% growth per year, its economy began to recover in 1999,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Tho20035-320] and grew at around 7% per year from 2000 to 2005, one of the fastest in the world.[^cite_note-Pincus2015-326][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQuang_Vinh13-327] On 11 January 2007, Vietnam became the 150th member of the [WTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) (World Trade Organization).[^cite_note-328] According to the [General Statistics Office of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam) (GSO), growth remained strong despite the [late-2000s global recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late-2000s_global_recession), holding at 6.8% in 2010. Vietnam's year-on-year inflation rate reached 11.8% in December 2010, and the currency, the [Vietnamese đồng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_%C4%91%E1%BB%93ng), was devalued three times.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAsian_Development_Bank2010388-329][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThanh_Nien2010-330]

Deep [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty), defined as the percentage of the population living on less than \$1 per day, has declined significantly in Vietnam, and the relative poverty rate is now less than that of China, India, and the [Philippines](23440.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVierraVierra20115-331] This decline can be attributed to [equitable economic policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equity_\(economics\)) aimed at improving [living standards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Living_standards) and preventing the rise of [inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_inequality).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVandemoorteleBird2010-332] These policies have included egalitarian land distribution during the initial stages of the *Đổi Mới* programme, investment in poorer remote areas, and subsidising of education and healthcare.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECuong_LeKuboFujinoMinh_Pham201023-333][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEH._DangGlewwe20179-334] Since the early 2000s, Vietnam has applied sequenced trade liberalisation, a two-track approach opening some sectors of the economy to international markets.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVandemoorteleBird2010-332][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVandemoortele2010-335] Manufacturing, [information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology) and high-tech industries now form a large and fast-growing part of the national economy. Although Vietnam is a relative newcomer to the [oil industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_industry), it is the third-largest oil producer in Southeast Asia with a total 2011 output of 318,000 barrels per day (50,600 m<sup>3</sup>/d).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUPI.com2013-336] In 2010, Vietnam was ranked as the eighth-largest crude [petroleum](23195.md) producer in the Asia and Pacific region.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFong-Sam201026-337] The US bought the biggest share of Vietnam's exports,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Viet_Nam_News''2018b-338] while [goods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goods) from China were the most popular Vietnamese import.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVietnam_News_Agency2018-339]

Based on findings by the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) (IMF) in 2022, the [unemployment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment) rate in Vietnam was 2.3%, the [nominal GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nominal_GDP) US\$406.452 billion, and a nominal [GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP_per_capita) \$4,086.[^cite_note-IMFWEO.VN-9][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETuoi_Tre_News2012-340] Besides the [primary sector economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_sector_of_the_economy), [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Vietnam) has contributed significantly to Vietnam's economic growth with 7.94 million foreign visitors recorded in 2015.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_Net''2016a-341]

### Agriculture

Photograph of terraced rice fields in Sa Pa

&#x20;Terraced rice fields in [Sa Pa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sa_Pa)

As a result of several [market liberalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_liberalization) measures, Vietnam has become a major exporter of agricultural products. It is now the world's largest producer of [cashew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cashew) nuts, with a one-third global share;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMai2017-342] the largest producer of [black pepper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_pepper), accounting for one-third of the world's market;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVoice_of_Vietnam2018c-343] and the second-largest [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) exporter in the world after [Thailand](30128.md) since the 1990s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENielsen20071-344] Subsequently, Vietnam is also the world's second largest exporter of [coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESummers2014-345] The country has the highest proportion of land use for [permanent crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_crop) together with other states in the [Greater Mekong Subregion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Mekong_Subregion).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETruongVoNguyen2018172-346] Other primary exports include [tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea), [rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubber) and fishery products. Agriculture's share of Vietnam's GDP has fallen in recent decades, declining from 42% in 1989 to 20% in 2006 as production in other sectors of the economy has risen.

#### Seafood

The overall fisheries production of Vietnam from capture fisheries and [aquaculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquaculture) was 5.6 million MT in 2011 and 6.7 million MT in 2016. The output of Vietnam's fisheries sector has seen strong growth, which could be attributed to the continued expansion of the aquaculture sub-sector.[^cite_note-347]

### Science and technology

Main article: [Science and technology in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Vietnam)

Photograph of a TOPIO humanoid ping-pong-playing robot

&#x20;A Vietnamese-made [TOPIO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TOPIO) 3.0 [humanoid](48180.md) [ping-pong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ping-pong)-playing robot displayed during the 2009 [International Robot Exhibition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Robot_Exhibition) (IREX) in Tokyo

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDigInfo2007-348]

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBorel2010-349]

In 2010, Vietnam's total state spending on science and technology amounted to roughly 0.45% of its GDP.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Viet_Nam_News''2010-350] Vietnamese scientists have made many significant contributions in various fields of study, most notably in [mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathematics). [Hoàng Tụy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoang_Tuy) pioneered the [applied mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Applied_mathematics) field of [global optimisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_optimisation) in the 20th century,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKoblitz2009198-351] while [Ngô Bảo Châu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ng%C3%B4_B%E1%BA%A3o_Ch%C3%A2u) won the 2010 [Fields Medal](10859.md) for his proof of [fundamental lemma in the theory of automorphic forms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundamental_lemma_\(Langlands_program\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECNRS2010-352][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKoppes2010-353] Since the establishment of the [Vietnam Academy of Science and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_Academy_of_Science_and_Technology) (VAST) by the government in 1975, the country is working to develop its first national [space flight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_flight) programme especially after the completion of the infrastructure at the Vietnam Space Centre (VSC) in 2018.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVietnam_National_Space_Centre2016-354][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVietnam_Academy_of_Science_and_Technology2017-355] Vietnam has also made significant advances in the development of [robots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robot), such as the [TOPIO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TOPIO) humanoid model.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDigInfo2007-348][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBorel2010-349] One of Vietnam's main [messaging apps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Messaging_apps), [Zalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zalo), was developed by Vương Quang Khải, a Vietnamese [hacker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hacker) who later worked with the country's largest [information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology) service company, the [FPT Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FPT_Group).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERaslan2017-356]

Vietnamese science students working on an experiment in their university lab.

Vietnamese science students working on an [experiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Experiment) in their university lab

According to the [UNESCO Institute for Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Institute_for_Statistics), Vietnam devoted 0.19% of its GDP to science research and development in 2011.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNESCO_Media_Services2016-357] Vietnam was ranked 44th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025. It has increased its ranking considerably since 2012, when it was ranked 76th.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360] Between 2005 and 2014, the number of Vietnamese scientific publications recorded in Thomson Reuters' [Web of Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_of_Science) increased at a rate well above the average for Southeast Asia, albeit from a modest starting point.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNESCO_Publishing713–714-361] Publications focus mainly on [life sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_science) (22%), [physics](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/physic) (13%), and [engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engineering) (13%), which is consistent with recent advances in the production of diagnostic equipment and shipbuilding.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNESCO_Publishing713–714-361]

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Vietnam)

Photograph of Hội An, a UNESCO World Heritage Site and a major tourist destination

[Hội An](2016839.md), a [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Educational,_Scientific_and_Cultural_Organization) [World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Southeast_Asia), is a major tourist destination. The photo is [Japanese Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Bridge).&#x20;

Tourism is an important element of economic activity in the nation, contributing 7.5% of the total GDP. Vietnam hosted roughly 13 million tourists in 2017, an increase of 29.1% over the previous year, making it one of the fastest-growing tourist destinations in the world. The vast majority of the tourists in the country, some 9.7 million, came from Asia; namely China (4 million), [South Korea](27019.md) (2.6 million), and Japan (798,119).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_National_Administration_of_Tourism''2018-362] Vietnam also attracts large numbers of visitors from Europe, with almost 1.9 million visitors in 2017; most European visitors came from Russia (574,164), followed by the United Kingdom (283,537), France (255,396), and Germany (199,872). Other significant international arrivals by nationality include the United States (614,117) and Australia (370,438).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_National_Administration_of_Tourism''2018-362]

The most visited destinations in Vietnam are the largest city, Ho Chi Minh City, with over 5.8 million international arrivals, followed by Hanoi with 4.6 million and [Hạ Long](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%E1%BA%A1_Long), including Hạ Long Bay with 4.4 million arrivals. All three are ranked in the top 100 most-visited cities in the world.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQuy2018-363] Vietnam is home to eight [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](44940.md). In 2018, *[Travel + Leisure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travel_+_Leisure)* ranked [Hội An](2016839.md) as one of the world's top 15 best destinations to visit.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETerzian2018-364]

### Transport

Main articles: [Transport in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Vietnam), [Rail transport in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Vietnam), and [List of airports in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Vietnam)

Much of Vietnam's modern transportation network can trace its roots to the French colonial era, when it was used to facilitate the transportation of [raw materials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raw_material) to its main ports. It was extensively expanded and modernised following the partition of Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECrook20147-365] Vietnam's road system includes national roads administered at the central level, provincial roads managed at the provincial level, district roads managed at the district level, urban roads managed by cities and towns, and commune roads managed at the commune level.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''General_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam''2010-366] In 2010, Vietnam's road system had a total length of about 188,744 kilometres (117,280 mi) of which 93,535 kilometres (58,120 mi) are [asphalt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asphalt_concrete) roads comprising national, provincial and district roads.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''General_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam''2010-366] The length of the national road system is about 15,370 kilometres (9,550 mi) with 15,085 kilometres (9,373 mi) of its length paved. The provincial road system has around 27,976 kilometres (17,383 mi) of paved roads, while 50,474 kilometres (31,363 mi) district roads are paved.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''General_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam''2010-366]

Photograph of Ho Chi Minh City's North–South Expressway

&#x20;[HCMC–LT–DG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ho_Chi_Minh_City%E2%80%93Long_Thanh%E2%80%93Dau_Giay_Expressway) section of the [North–South Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North%E2%80%93South_Expressway_East)

Photograph of Tan Son Nhat International Airport

&#x20;[Tan Son Nhat International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tan_Son_Nhat_International_Airport) is the busiest airport in the country.

Bicycles, motorcycles, and [motor scooters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motor_scooter) remain the most popular forms of road transport in the country, a legacy of the French, though the number of privately owned cars has been increasing in recent years.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuu_DucMai_HoaThien_HuongNgoc_Bao20132080-367] Public buses operated by private companies are the main mode of long-distance travel for much of the population. [Traffic collisions](19740545.md) remain the major safety issue of Vietnamese transportation, with an average of 30 people losing their lives daily.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''General_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam''2011-368] [Traffic congestion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_congestion) is a growing problem in both Hanoi and Ho Chi Minh City, especially with the growth of individual car ownership.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELinh_LeAnh_Trinh2016-369][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESohrBrockfeldSauerlanderMelde2016220-370] Vietnam's primary cross-country rail service is the [Reunification Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North%E2%80%93South_railway_\(Vietnam\)) from Ho Chi Minh City to Hanoi, a distance of nearly 1,726 kilometres (1,072 mi).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChin2018-371] From Hanoi, railway lines branch out to the northeast, north, and west; the eastbound line runs from Hanoi to Hạ Long Bay, the northbound line from Hanoi to [Thái Nguyên](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%A1i_Nguy%C3%AAn_province), and the northeast line from Hanoi to Lào Cai. In 2009, Vietnam and Japan signed a deal to build a high-speed railway—[shinkansen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shinkansen) (bullet train)—using Japanese technology.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_Japan_Times''2009-372] Vietnamese engineers were sent to Japan to receive training in the operation and maintenance of high-speed trains.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVietnam+2008-373] The planned railway will be a 1,545 kilometres (960 mi)-long express route serving a total of 23 stations, including Hanoi and Ho Chi Minh City, with 70% of its route running on bridges and through tunnels.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_New_York_Times''2018-374][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_Net''2018b-375] The trains will travel at a maximum speed of 350 kilometres (220 mi) per hour.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_Net''2018b-375][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouth_East_Asia_Iron_and_Steel_Institute2009-376] Plans for the high-speed rail line, however, have been postponed after the Vietnamese government decided to prioritise the development of both the [Hanoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanoi_Metro) and [Ho Chi Minh City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ho_Chi_Minh_City_Metro) metros and expand road networks instead.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChin2018-371][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChi2017-377][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETatarski2017-378]

Photograph of a crane at the Port of Hai Phong

&#x20;The port of [Hai Phong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hai_Phong) is one of the largest and busiest container ports in Vietnam.

Vietnam operates 20 major civil airports, including three international gateways: [Noi Bai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noi_Bai) in Hanoi, [Da Nang International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Da_Nang_International_Airport) in Đà Nẵng, and [Tan Son Nhat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tan_Son_Nhat) in Ho Chi Minh City. Tan Son Nhat is the country's largest airport, handling the majority of international passenger traffic.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoang20161-379] According to a government-approved plan, Vietnam will have another seven international airports by 2025, including [Vinh International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinh_International_Airport), [Phu Bai International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phu_Bai_International_Airport), [Cam Ranh International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cam_Ranh_International_Airport), [Phu Quoc International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phu_Quoc_International_Airport), [Cat Bi International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cat_Bi_International_Airport), [Can Tho International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Can_Tho_International_Airport), and [Long Thanh International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Thanh_International_Airport). The planned Long Thanh International Airport will have an annual service capacity of 100 million passengers once it becomes fully operational in 2025.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_Investment_Review''2018-380] [Vietnam Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_Airlines), the state-owned national airline, maintains a fleet of 86 passenger aircraft and aims to operate 170 by 2020.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaGiangDenslow2012-381] Several private airlines also operate in Vietnam, including [Air Mekong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Mekong), [Bamboo Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamboo_Airways), [Jetstar Pacific Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jetstar_Pacific_Airlines), [VASCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_Air_Service_Company) and [VietJet Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VietJet_Air). As a coastal country, Vietnam has many major sea ports, including [Cam Ranh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cam_Ranh), Đà Nẵng, [Hải Phòng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haiphong), Ho Chi Minh City, [Hạ Long](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%E1%BA%A1_Long), [Qui Nhơn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qui_Nh%C6%A1n), [Vũng Tàu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C5%A9ng_T%C3%A0u), [Cửa Lò](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%E1%BB%ADa_L%C3%B2), and [Nha Trang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nha_Trang). Further inland, the country's extensive network of rivers plays a key role in rural transportation with over 47,130 kilometres (29,290 mi) of navigable [waterways](174851.md) carrying ferries, barges, and [water taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_taxi).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIndex_Mundi2018-382]

### Energy

Main articles: [Energy in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Vietnam) and [List of power stations in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_power_stations_in_Vietnam)

Photograph of the Sơn La Dam

&#x20;[Sơn La Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C6%A1n_La_Dam) in northern Vietnam, the [largest hydroelectric dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_hydroelectric_power_stations) in Southeast Asia

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Intellasia''2010-383]

Vietnam's energy sector is dominated largely by the state-controlled [Vietnam Electricity Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_Electricity) (EVN). As of 2017, EVN made up about 61.4% of the country's power generation system with a total power capacity of 25,884 [MW](21347693.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEElectricity_of_Vietnam201710-384] Other energy sources are [PetroVietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PetroVietnam) (4,435 MW), [Vinacomin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinacomin) (1,785 MW) and 10,031 MW from [build–operate–transfer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Build%E2%80%93operate%E2%80%93transfer) (BOT) investors.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEElectricity_of_Vietnam201712-385]

Most of Vietnam's power is generated by either [hydropower](14073.md) or [fossil fuel power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel_power_station) such as coal, oil and [gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_gas_in_Vietnam), while [diesel](81761.md), [small hydropower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_hydropower) and renewable energy supplies the remainder.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEElectricity_of_Vietnam201712-385] The Vietnamese government had planned to develop a [nuclear reactor](22151.md) as the path to establish [another source for electricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_energy_in_Vietnam) from [nuclear power](22153.md). The plan was abandoned in late 2016 when a majority of the National Assembly voted to oppose the project due to widespread public concern over [radioactive contamination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radioactive_contamination).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENguyenBinh_MinhPhamBurmistrova2016-386]

The household gas sector in Vietnam is dominated by PetroVietnam, which controls nearly 70% of the country's domestic market for [liquefied petroleum gas](361384.md) (LPG).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Nikkei_Asian_Review''-387] Since 2011, the company has also operated five renewable energy power plants, including the Nhơn Trạch 2 Thermal Power Plant (750 MW), Phú Quý Wind Power Plant (6 MW), Hủa Na Hydro-power Plant (180 MW), Dakdrinh Hydro-power Plant (125 MW), and Vũng Áng 1 Thermal Power Plant (1,200 MW).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViet_TrungQuoc_VietVan_Chat201670-388]

According to statistics from [BP](18998720.md), Vietnam is listed among the 52 countries that have [proven crude oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves). In 2015, the reserve was approximately 4.4 billion barrels, ranking Vietnam in first place in Southeast Asia, while the [proven gas reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_proven_reserves) were about 0.6 trillion cubic metres (tcm) and ranking it third in Southeast Asia after [Indonesia](14579.md) and [Malaysia](3607937.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViet_TrungQuoc_VietVan_Chat201664-389]

### Telecommunication

Main article: [Telecommunications in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Vietnam)

Telecommunications services in Vietnam are wholly provided by the Vietnam Post and Telecommunications General Corporation (now the [VNPT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VNPT) Group), which is a [state-owned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-owned) company.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPham20156-390] The VNPT retained its monopoly until 1986. The telecom sector was reformed in 1995 when the Vietnamese government began to implement a competitive policy with the creation of two domestic telecommunication companies, the Military Electronic and Telecommunication Company ([Viettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viettel), which is wholly owned by the Vietnamese Ministry of Defence) and the Saigon Post and Telecommunication Company (SPT or SaigonPostel), with 18% of it owned by VNPT.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPham20156-390] VNPT's monopoly was finally ended by the government in 2003 with the issuance of a decree.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPham20157-391] By 2012, the top three telecom operators in Vietnam were Viettel, [Vinaphone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinaphone), and [MobiFone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MobiFone). The remaining companies included: [EVNTelecom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EVNTelecom), Vietnammobile and [S-Fone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-Fone).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Viet_Nam_News''2012-392] With the shift towards a more [market-oriented economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_economy), Vietnam's telecommunications market is continuously being reformed to attract [foreign investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment), which includes the supply of services and the establishment of nationwide telecom infrastructure.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Oxford_Business_Group''2017-393]

### Water supply and sanitation

Main article: [Water supply and sanitation in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Vietnam)

Stream flowing down a hill with a bridge crossing it

&#x20;In rural areas of Vietnam, [piped water systems](684928.md) are operated by a wide variety of institutions including a national organisation, people committees (local government), community groups, co-operatives and private companies.

Vietnam has 2,360 rivers with an average annual discharge of 310 billion [m<sup>3</sup>](30125638.md). The rainy season accounts for 70% of the year's discharge.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''British_Business_Group_Vietnam''20171-394] Most of the country's urban [water supply](684928.md) systems have been developed without proper management within the last 10 years. Based on a 2008 survey by the Vietnam Water Supply and Sewerage Association (VWSA), existing water production capacity exceeded demand, but service coverage is still sparse. Most of the clean water supply infrastructure is not widely developed. It is only available to a small proportion of the population, with about one-third of 727 district towns having some form of piped water supply.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''British_Business_Group_Vietnam''20172-395] There is also concern over the safety of existing water resources for urban and rural water supply systems. Most industrial factories release their untreated [wastewater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wastewater) directly into the water sources. Where the government does not take measures to address the issue, most domestic wastewater is discharged, untreated, back into the environment and pollutes the [surface water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surface_water).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''British_Business_Group_Vietnam''20172-395]

In recent years, there have been some efforts and collaboration between local and foreign universities to develop access to safe water in the country by introducing [water filtration systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_filter). There is a growing concern among local populations over the serious public health issues associated with water contamination caused by pollution as well as the [high levels of arsenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arsenic_contamination_of_groundwater) in [groundwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groundwater) sources.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUniversity_of_Technology_Sydney2018-396] The government of Netherlands has been providing aid focusing its investments mainly on water-related sectors including [water treatment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_treatment) projects.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_the_Netherlands''2016-397][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_the_Netherlands''2018-398][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnh2018-399] Regarding [sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanitation), 78% of Vietnam's population has access to ["improved" sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improved_sanitation)—94% of the urban population and 70% of the rural population. However, there are still about 21 million people in the country lacking access to "improved" sanitation according to a survey conducted in 2015.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNICEF2015-400] In 2018, the construction ministry said the country's water supply and drainage industry had been applying hi-tech methods and [information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology) (IT) to sanitation issues but faced problems like limited funding, climate change, and pollution.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Viet_Nam_News''2018c-401] The health ministry has also announced that water inspection units will be established nationwide beginning in June 2019. Inspections are to be conducted without notice, since there have been many cases involving health issues caused by poor or polluted water supplies, as well as unhygienic conditions reported every year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Viet_Nam_News''2018d-402]

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Vietnam](32379.md) and [List of ethnic groups in Vietnam](153139.md)



Vietnam population pyramid in 2019

Ethnic groups of Vietnam

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJones199821-403]

1. [Viet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viet_people) (85.3%)

2. [Other](153139.md) (14.7%)

As of 2021, the population of Vietnam stands at approximately 97.5 million people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_Department_of_Economic_and_Social_Affairs-404] The population had grown significantly from the 1979 census, which showed the total population of reunified Vietnam to be 52.7 million.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFraser1980-405] According to the 2019 census, the country's population was 96,208,984.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGeneral_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam2019-2] Based on the 2019 census, 65.6% of the Vietnamese population lives in rural areas while only 34.4% live in urban areas. The average growth rate of the urban population has recently increased, which is attributed mainly to migration and rapid urbanisation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGeneral_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam2019-2] The dominant [Viet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viet_people) or Kinh ethnic group constitute 82,085,826 people or 85.32% of the population.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGeneral_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam2019-2] Most of their population is concentrated in the country's [alluvial deltas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alluvial_fans) and coastal plains. As the majority ethnic group, the Kinh possess significant political and economic influence over the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJones199821-403] Despite this, Vietnam is also home to various ethnic groups, of which [54 are officially recognised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Vietnam), including the [Tày](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A0y_people), [Thái](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%A1i_people_\(Vietnam\)), [Nùng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%B9ng_people), [Hmong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hmong_people), and [Dao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yao_people_\(Asia\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Citizenship_and_Immigration''20131-406] Many ethnic minorities, including the [Mường](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muong_people) who are closely related to the Kinh, dwell in the highlands, which cover two-thirds of Vietnam's territory.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_Vietnam_(I)''-407]

Before the 20th century, the [Central Highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Highlands_\(Vietnam\)) was predominantly inhabited by the indigenous [Montagnard peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montagnard_\(Vietnam\)), comprising more than 40 tribal groups. The South Vietnamese government later enacted a programme of resettling Kinh in the areas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECultural_Orientation_Resource_Centre7-408][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontagnard_Human_Rights_Organisation-409] The [Hoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoa_people) (ethnic [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Chinese)), [Cham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chams), and [Khmer Krom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmer_Krom) people are mainly lowlanders.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJones199821-403][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKoskoff20081316-410] Throughout Vietnam's history, many Chinese people, largely from [South China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China), migrated to the country as administrators, merchants, and even refugees.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDoddLewis2003531-411] Since the reunification in 1976, an increase in communist policies nationwide resulted in the nationalisation and confiscation of property, especially from the Hoa in the south and the wealthy in cities. This led many of them to leave Vietnam.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmer1996-412][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFeinberg2016-413]

### Urbanisation

See also: [List of cities in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Vietnam)

Ho Chi Minh City's District 1 skyline photographed at night

&#x20;[District 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_1,_Ho_Chi_Minh_City), Ho Chi Minh City

The number of people who live in urbanised areas in 2019 was 33,122,548 people (with the urbanisation rate at 34.4%).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGeneral_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam2019-2] Since 1986, Vietnam's urbanisation rates have surged rapidly after the Vietnamese government implemented the Đổi Mới economic programme, changing the system into a socialist one and liberalising property rights. As a result, Hanoi and Ho Chi Minh City (the two major cities in the Red River Delta and Southeast regions, respectively) increased their share of the total urban population from 8.5% and 24.9% to 15.9% and 31% respectively.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_Population_Fund2009117-414] The Vietnamese government, through its [construction ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Construction_\(Vietnam\)), forecasts the country will have a 45% urbanisation rate by 2020, although it was confirmed to only be 34.4% according to the 2019 census.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGeneral_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam2019-2] Urbanisation is said to have a positive correlation with economic growth. Any country with higher urbanisation rates has a higher GDP growth rate.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank2002-415] Furthermore, the urbanisation movement in Vietnam is mainly between the rural areas and the country's Southeast region. Ho Chi Minh City has received a large number of migrants due mainly to better weather and economic opportunities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_Population_Fund2009102-416]

A study also shows that rural-to-urban area migrants have a higher standard of living than both non-migrants in rural areas and non-migrants in urban areas. This results in changes to economic structures. In 1985, agriculture made up 37.2% of Vietnam's GDP; in 2008, that number had declined to 18.5%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECiraDasturKilroyLozano2011194-417] In 1985, industry made up only 26.2% of Vietnam's GDP; by 2008, that number had increased to 43.2%. Urbanisation also helps to improve basic services, which increase people's standards of living. Access to electricity grew from 14% of total households with electricity in 1993 to above 96% in 2009.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECiraDasturKilroyLozano2011194-417] In terms of access to fresh water, data from 65 utility companies show that only 12% of households in the area covered by them had access to the water network in 2002; by 2007, more than 70% of the population was connected. Though urbanisation has many benefits, it has some drawbacks since it creates more traffic, and air and water pollution.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECiraDasturKilroyLozano2011194-417]

Many Vietnamese use [mopeds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moped) for transportation, since they are relatively cheap and easy to operate. Their large numbers have been known to cause traffic congestion and air pollution in Vietnam. In the capital city alone, the number of mopeds increased from 0.5 million in 2001 to 4.7 million in 2013.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECiraDasturKilroyLozano2011194-417] With rapid development, factories have sprung up which indirectly pollute the air and water, for example, in the [2016 Vietnam marine life disaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Vietnam_marine_life_disaster).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETiezzi2016-418] The government is intervening and attempting solutions to decrease air pollution by decreasing the number of motorcycles while increasing public transportation. It has introduced more regulations for [waste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waste) handling. The amount of solid waste generated in urban areas of Vietnam has increased by more than 200% from 2003 to 2008. Industrial solid waste accounted for 181% of that increase. One of the government's efforts includes attempting to promote campaigns that encourage locals to sort [household waste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_solid_waste), since [waste sorting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waste_sorting) is still not practised by most of Vietnamese society.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETruong201819-419]&#x20;
