### Languages

The [national language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_language) of the country is [Vietnamese](32511.md), a tonal [Vietic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietic_languages) language in the [Austroasiatic language family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austroasiatic_languages), which is spoken by the majority of the population.[^cite_note-423] Vietnam's minority groups speak a variety of languages, including: [Tày](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A0y_language), [Nùng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%B9ng_language_\(Tai\)), [Mường](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Dng_language), [Cham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cham_language), [Khmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmer_language), Chinese (largely [Cantonese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonese), [Teochew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teochew_Min), and [Hakka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakka_Chinese)), and [Hmong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hmong_language). The [Montagnard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montagnard_\(Vietnam\)) peoples of the [Central Highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Highlands_\(Vietnam\)) also speak many distinct languages, some belonging to the Austroasiatic and others to the [Malayo-Polynesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayo-Polynesian_languages) language families.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECultural_Orientation_Resource_Centre10-424] In recent years, a number of [sign languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_sign_languages) have developed in the major cities.

A sample of traditional Vietnamese calligraphy

&#x20;[Vietnamese calligraphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_calligraphy) in Latin alphabet

The French language, a legacy of colonial rule, is spoken by some educated Vietnamese as a second language, especially among those educated in the former [South Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Vietnam), where it was a principal language in administration, education, and commerce.[^cite_note-425] Vietnam remains a full member of the [International Organisation of the Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie) (*La Francophonie*) and education has revived some interest in the language.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''French_Senate''1997-426] [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language), and to a lesser extent German, [Czech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_language), and [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language) are known among some northern Vietnamese whose families had ties with the Eastern Bloc during the Cold War.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Van8-427] With improved relations with Western countries and recent reforms in Vietnamese administration, English has been increasingly used as a second language, and the study of English is now obligatory in most schools either alongside or in place of French.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Van9-428][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Government_of_the_United_Kingdom''2018-429] The popularity of Japanese, [Korean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_language), and [Mandarin Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandarin_Chinese) has also grown as the country's ties with other East Asian nations have strengthened.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWai-ming20023-430][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnh_Dinh201663-431][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHirano2016-432] Third-graders can choose one of seven languages (English, Russian, French, Chinese, Japanese, Korean, German) as their first foreign language.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435] In Vietnam's [high school graduation examinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Vietnam), students can take their foreign language exam in one of the above-mentioned languages.[^cite_note-436]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Vietnam)

Religion in Vietnam (2021)

[^cite_note-White_rel-3]

1. [Vietnamese folk religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_folk_religion) or no religion (73.0%)

2. [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Vietnam) (14.3%)

3. [Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Vietnam) (7.10%)

4. [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_Vietnam) (1.20%)

5. [Hoahaoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%B2a_H%E1%BA%A3o) (1.50%)

6. [Caodaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caodaism) (1.20%)

7. Islam (0.08%)

8. Others (1.62%)

Article 70 of the 1992 Constitution of Vietnam declares [freedom of belief and religion](46006.md) for all citizens.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Justice''1992-437] Accordingly, [places of worship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_of_worship) are protected under Vietnamese law, while religious beliefs and practices are subject to laws and regulations governing religion.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Justice''2004b-438][^cite_note-439] According to a 2007 survey 81% of Vietnamese people [did not believe in a god](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZuckerman200711-440] Based on government findings in 2009, the number of religious people increased by 932,000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''General_Statistics_Office_of_Vietnam''2009-441] The official statistics, presented by the Vietnamese government to the [United Nations special rapporteur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_special_rapporteur) in 2014, indicate the overall number of followers of recognised religions is about 24 million of a total population of almost 90 million.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBielefeldt2014-442]

According to the white paper *Religions and Religious Policy in Vietnam* (2023), in 2021 Vietnam had 36 denominational organizations belonging to 16 religions that had received state recognition and registration to operate, with 26.5 million adherents accounting for 27% of the population.[^cite_note-White_rel-3][^cite_note-443] According to the white paper, [Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Vietnam) accounted for 14.3% of the total population, [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Vietnam) 7.1%, [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_Vietnam) 1.2%, [Hoahaoist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%B2a_H%E1%BA%A3o) 1.5%, and [Caodaists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caodaism) 1.2%. Other government-recognized religions include [Tịnh độ cư sĩ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%E1%BB%8Bnh_%C4%91%E1%BB%99_c%C6%B0_s%C4%A9?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Islam](6037917.md), [Baháʼí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith), [Adventists](28632.md), [Tứ Ân Hiếu Nghĩa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%E1%BB%A9_%C3%82n_Hi%E1%BA%BFu_Ngh%C4%A9a), [Minh Sư đạo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minh_S%C6%B0_%C4%91%E1%BA%A1o?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Minh Lý đạo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minh_L%C3%BD_%C4%91%E1%BA%A1o?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism), [Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latter-day_Saints), [Bửu Sơn Kỳ Hương](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%E1%BB%ADu_S%C6%A1n_K%E1%BB%B3_H%C6%B0%C6%A1ng), and [Hiếu Nghĩa Tà Lơn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hi%E1%BA%BFu_Ngh%C4%A9a_T%C3%A0_L%C6%A1n?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-White_rel-3]

The majority of Vietnamese do not follow any organised religion, though many of them observe some form of [Vietnamese folk religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_folk_religion) and elements of Buddhism.[^cite_note-Luat_khoa-444] [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism) as a system of social and ethical philosophy still has certain influences in modern Vietnam. [Mahāyāna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mah%C4%81y%C4%81na_Buddhism) is the dominant branch of Buddhism, while [Theravada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theravada) is practised mostly by the Khmer minority. Due to the ambiguity of what constitutes being Buddhist and the influence of Buddhism on Vietnamese culture, estimates of the Buddhist population vary widely, with the [Vietnam Buddhist Sangha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_Buddhist_Sangha) claiming about 10 million adherents in 2010.[^cite_note-Luat_khoa-444] About 8 to 9% of the population is Christian, made up of Catholics and Protestants. Catholicism was introduced to Vietnam in the 16th century and was firmly established by [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuits) missionaries (mainly Portuguese and Italian) in the 17th century from nearby [Portuguese Macau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Macau).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETran2018-88] French missionaries (from the [Paris Foreign Missions Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Foreign_Missions_Society)) and Spanish missionaries (from the [Dominican Order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Order)) engaged in missionary work in the 18th, 19th, and first half of the 20th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoods200234-445][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKeith201242,_72-446][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELamport2018898-447] A significant number of Vietnamese people, especially in the South, are also adherents of two indigenous religions of syncretic [Caodaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caodaism) and semi-Buddhist [Hoahaoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%B2a_H%E1%BA%A3o).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELargo2002168-448] Protestantism was spread by Canadian and American missionaries in the 20th century;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Hoang20171-449] the largest Protestant denomination is the Evangelical Church of Vietnam. Around 770,000 of the country's Protestants are members of ethnic minorities,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Hoang20171-449] particularly the highland [Montagnards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montagnard_\(Vietnam\))[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECultural_Orientation_Resource_Centre5,_7-450] and [Hmong people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hmong_people). Protestantism has experienced significant growth in Vietnam in the early 21st century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Hoang20171-449][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''United_States_Department_of_State''2005-451] Several other minority faiths exist in Vietnam; these include [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam), [non-denominational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-denominational_Muslim), and syncretic Bani schools of Islam, which are practised primarily among the ethnic [Cham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chams) minority.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKy_PhuongLockhart201135-452] There are also a few Kinh adherents of Islam, other minority adherents of [Baháʼí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith_in_Vietnam), as well as [Hindus among the Cham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Southeast_Asia#Vietnam).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELevinsonChristensen200289-453][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESharma200948-454]

### Education

Main article: [Education in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Vietnam)

Vietnam has an extensive state-controlled network of schools, colleges, and universities, and a growing number of privately run and partially privatised institutions. General education in Vietnam is divided into five categories: [kindergarten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kindergarten), [elementary schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elementary_school), [middle schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_school), [high schools](554992.md), and [universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universities). A large number of public schools have been constructed across the country to raise the national [literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy_rate), which stood at 90% in 2008.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNICEF-455] Most universities are located in major cities of Hanoi and Ho Chi Minh City, with the country's education system continuously undergoing a series of reforms by the government. [Basic education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_education) in the country is relatively free for the poor, although some families may still have trouble paying tuition fees for their children without some form of public or private assistance.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHa_Tran2014-456] Regardless, Vietnam's school enrolment is among the highest in the world.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank2013-457][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank2015-458] The number of colleges and universities increased dramatically in the 2000s from 178 in 2000 to 299 in 2005. In higher education, the government provides subsidised loans for students through the national bank, although there are deep concerns about access to the loans as well as the burden on students to repay them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPham2012-459][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChapmanLiu2013-460] Since 1995, enrolment in higher education has grown tenfold to over 2.2 million with 84,000 lecturers and 419 institutions of higher education.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEde_MoraWood201455-461] Many foreign universities operate private campuses in Vietnam, including [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) (United States) and the [Royal Melbourne Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Melbourne_Institute_of_Technology_Vietnam) (Australia). The government's strong commitment to education has fostered significant growth, but it still needs to be sustained to retain academics. In 2018, a decree on university autonomy allowing them to operate independently without ministerial control was in its final stages of approval. The government will continue investing in education, especially for the poor, to have access to basic education.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_Net''2016b-462]

### Health

Main article: [Health in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Vietnam)



Development of life expectancy in Vietnam since 1950

By 2015, 97% of the population had access to improved water sources.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIndex_Mundi2016-463] In 2016, Vietnam's national [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) stood at 80.9 years for women and 71.5 for men, and the [infant mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality) rate was 17 per 1,000 live births.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank2016b-464] Since the partition, North Vietnam has established a public health system that has reached down to the [hamlet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamlet_\(place\)) level.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_Harvard_Crimson''1972-465] After the national reunification in 1975, a nationwide health service was established.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWagstaffvan_DoorslaerWatanabe2003-201] In the late 1980s, the quality of healthcare declined to some degree as a result of budgetary constraints, a shift of responsibility to the provinces, and the introduction of charges.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECuong_LeKuboFujinoMinh_Pham201023-333] Inadequate funding has also contributed to a shortage of [nurses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nursing), [midwives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwives) and hospital beds; in 2000, Vietnam had only 24.7 hospital beds per 10,000 people before declining to 23.7 in 2005 as stated in the annual report of [Vietnamese Health Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_\(Vietnam\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETrung_Chien200665-466] The controversial use of herbicides as a chemical weapon by the [US military](32212.md) during the war left tangible, long-term [impacts upon the Vietnamese people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Effects_of_Agent_Orange_on_the_Vietnamese_people) that persist in the country today.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News2005-467][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaberman2014-468] For instance, it led to three million Vietnamese people suffering health problems, one million birth defects caused directly by exposure to the chemical, and 24% of Vietnam's land being defoliated.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGustafsson2010125-469]

Since the early 2000s, Vietnam has made significant progress in combating [malaria](20423.md). The malaria mortality rate fell to about five per cent of its 1990s equivalent by 2005 after the country introduced improved antimalarial drugs and treatment.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Namde_VriesVan_ToiNagelkerke2005-470] [Tuberculosis](30653.md) (TB) cases, however, are on the rise. TB has become the second most infectious disease in the country after [respiratory-related illness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_disease).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETrinhNguyenDoNguyen2016-471] With an intensified vaccination programme, better hygiene, and foreign assistance, Vietnam hopes to reduce sharply the number of TB cases and new TB infections.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcNeil2016-472] In 2004, government subsidies covered about 15% of health care expenses.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiebermanWagstaff200940-473] That year, the United States announced Vietnam would be one of 15 states to receive funding as part of its global AIDS relief plan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEManyin20054-474] By the following year, Vietnam had diagnosed 101,291 [human immunodeficiency virus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_immunodeficiency_virus) (HIV) cases, of which 16,528 progressed to [acquired immune deficiency syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acquired_immune_deficiency_syndrome) (AIDS); 9,554 had died.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVietnam_Women's_Union2005-475] The actual number of HIV-positive individuals is estimated to be much higher. On average, between 40 and 50 new infections are reported daily in the country. In 2007, 0.4% of the population was estimated to be infected with HIV, and the figure has remained stable since 2005.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank2018a-476] More global aid is being delivered through [The Global Fund to Fight AIDS, Tuberculosis and Malaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Global_Fund_to_Fight_AIDS,_Tuberculosis_and_Malaria) to fight the spread of the disease in the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcNeil2016-472] In September 2018, the Hanoi People's Committee urged the citizens of the country to stop eating [dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dog_meat) and [cat meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cat_meat) as it can cause diseases like [rabies](16946852.md) and [leptospirosis](649760.md). More than 1,000 stores in the capital city of Hanoi were found to be selling both meats. The decision prompted positive comments among Vietnamese on [social media](5897742.md), though some noted that the consumption of dog meat will remain an ingrained habit among many people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News2018-477]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Vietnam)

The Temple of Literature in Hanoi

The [Temple of Literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Literature,_Hanoi) in Hanoi

The Meridian Gate (Huế)

The [Imperial City of Huế](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_City_of_Hu%E1%BA%BF)

The Municipal Theatre in Ho Chi Minh City

The [Municipal Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Theatre,_Ho_Chi_Minh_City) (Saigon Opera House) in Ho Chi Minh City

Vietnamese culture is mainly regarded as part of the [Sinosphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinosphere). Vietnam's culture has developed over the centuries from the indigenous ancient [Đông Sơn culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%B4ng_S%C6%A1n_culture) with wet rice cultivation as its economic base.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHigham1984-48][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECalo200951-51] Many elements of the nation's culture have Chinese origins, drawing on elements of [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism), [Mahāyāna Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahayana), and [Taoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism) in its traditional political system and philosophy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETung_Hieu201571-478][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENhu_Nguyen201632-479] Historically, Vietnamese society has been structured around *làng* (villages).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEndres2001-480] Vietnamese mark a [common ancestral anniversary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gi%E1%BB%97_T%E1%BB%95_H%C3%B9ng_V%C6%B0%C6%A1ng) on the tenth day of the third [lunar month](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_calendar).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrigoreva20144-481][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage2012-482] The influence of [Chinese culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_culture) is more evident in the north, affecting both elite traditions and popular customs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhuRenCollinsWarner2017142-483] Nevertheless, there are several [Chinatowns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown) in the south, such as in [Chợ Lớn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%E1%BB%A3_L%E1%BB%9Bn), where many ethnic Chinese have [intermarried](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interracial_marriage) with Kinh and are no longer distinguishable from them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcLeodThi_Dieu20018-484] In the central and southern parts of Vietnam, traces of [Champa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champa) and [Khmer culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmer_culture) are evidenced through the remains of ruins, artefacts, as well as within their population as the successor of the ancient [Sa Huỳnh culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sa_Hu%E1%BB%B3nh_culture).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMomoki199636-485][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKy_PhuongLockhart201184-486] In recent centuries, Western cultures have become popular among recent generations of Vietnamese.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENhu_Nguyen201632-479]

Photograph of two girls wearing a traditional Vietnamese white school uniform, the áo dài—both are holding the nón lá, a conical hat

Vietnamese traditional white [school uniform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_uniforms_by_country#Vietnam) for girls in the country, the *[áo dài](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81o_d%C3%A0i)* with the addition of *[nón lá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_conical_hat)*, a conical hat

The traditional focuses of Vietnamese culture are based on humanity (*nhân nghĩa*) and harmony (*hòa*), in which family and community values are highly regarded.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhuRenCollinsWarner2017142-483] Vietnam reveres a number of key cultural symbols,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVo201296-487] such as the [Vietnamese dragon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_dragon) which is derived from [crocodile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crocodile) and [snake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snake) imagery; Vietnam's national father, [Lạc Long Quân](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%E1%BA%A1c_Long_Qu%C3%A2n) is depicted as a holy dragon.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrigoreva20144-481][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGallop2017-488][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVietnamese-American_Association-489] The *lạc* is a holy bird representing Vietnam's national mother [Âu Cơ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%82u_C%C6%A1). Other prominent images that are also revered are the [turtle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turtle), [buffalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_buffalo), and [horse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horse).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChonchirdsin2016-490] Many Vietnamese also believe in the [supernatural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supernatural) and [spiritualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spiritualism_\(beliefs\)) where illness can be brought on by a [curse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curse) or [sorcery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witchcraft) or caused by non-observance of a religious ethic. Traditional medical practitioners, [amulets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amulet), and other forms of spiritual protection and religious practices may be employed to treat the ill person.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaitemata_District_Health_Board20152-491] In the modern era, the cultural life of Vietnam has been deeply influenced by government-controlled media and cultural programmes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENhu_Nguyen201632-479] For many decades, foreign cultural influences, especially those of Western origin, were shunned. But since the recent reformation, Vietnam has seen a greater exposure to neighbouring Southeast Asian, East Asian, as well as Western culture and media.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPhuong2012-492]

The main Vietnamese formal dress, the *[áo dài](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81o_d%C3%A0i)*, is worn for special occasions such as weddings and religious festivals. White *áo dài* is the required uniform for girls in many high schools across the country. Other examples of traditional Vietnamese clothing include the *[áo tứ thân](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81o_t%E1%BB%A9_th%C3%A2n)*, a four-piece woman's dress; the *áo ngũ*, a form of the *thân* in five-piece form, mostly worn in the north of the country; the *[yếm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%E1%BA%BFm)*, a woman's undergarment; the *[áo bà ba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81o_b%C3%A0_ba)*, rural working "[pyjamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyjamas)" for men and women; the *[áo gấm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81o_g%E1%BA%A5m)*, a formal brocade tunic for government receptions; and the *áo the*, a variant of the *áo gấm* worn by grooms at weddings.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELewandowski201112-493][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward201690-494] Traditional headwear includes the standard conical *[nón lá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%B3n_l%C3%A1)*, the "lampshade-like" *[nón quai thao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%B3n_quai_thao)*, and the traditional turban, *[khăn vấn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kh%C4%83n_v%E1%BA%A5n)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoward201690-494][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChico2013354-495] In tourism, many popular cultural destinations include the former [Imperial City of Huế](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_City_of_Hu%E1%BA%BF), the [World Heritage Sites](44940.md) of [Phong Nha – Kẻ Bàng National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phong_Nha_%E2%80%93_K%E1%BA%BB_B%C3%A0ng_National_Park), [Hội An](2016839.md) and [Mỹ Sơn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%E1%BB%B9_S%C6%A1n), coastal regions such as Nha Trang, the caves of Hạ Long Bay and the [Marble Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marble_Mountains_\(Vietnam\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_Net''2017a-496]

### Literature

Main articles: [Vietnamese literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_literature), [Vietnamese poetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_poetry), [Vietnamese mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_mythology), and [Vietnamese fairy tales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_fairy_tales)

A scroll showing a Vietnamese dragon

[Vietnamese dragon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_dragon) on Emperor [Vĩnh Hựu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%AA_%C3%9D_T%C3%B4ng)'s c. 1736 edict conferring a title

Vietnamese literature has a centuries-deep history and the country has a rich tradition of [folk literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_literature) based on the typical–to-eight-verse poetic form (*[lục bát](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%E1%BB%A5c_b%C3%A1t)*) called *ca dao*, which usually focuses on village ancestors and heroes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuong2010-497] Written literature has been found dating back to the 10th century [Ngô dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ng%C3%B4_dynasty), with notable ancient authors including [Nguyễn Trãi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_Tr%C3%A3i), [Hồ Xuân Hương](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%E1%BB%93_Xu%C3%A2n_H%C6%B0%C6%A1ng), [Nguyễn Du](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_Du) and [Nguyễn Đình Chiểu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_%C4%90%C3%ACnh_Chi%E1%BB%83u). Some literary genres play an important role in theatrical performance, such as *hát nói* in *[ca trù](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ca_tr%C3%B9)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENorton2015-498] Some poetic unions have also been formed in Vietnam, such as the *tao đàn*. Vietnamese literature has been influenced by Western styles in recent times, with the first literary transformation movement of *[thơ mới](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C6%A1_m%E1%BB%9Bi)* emerging in 1932.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELe2008-499] Vietnamese folk literature is an intermingling of many forms. It is not only an oral tradition, but a mixing of three media: hidden (only retained in the memory of folk authors), fixed (written), and shown (performed). Folk literature usually exists in many versions, passed down orally, and has unknown authors. [Myths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myths) consist of stories about supernatural beings, heroes, and creator gods, and reflect the viewpoint of ancient people about human life.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVo20124-500] They consist of creation stories, stories about their origins (*Lạc Long Quân* and *Âu Cơ*), [culture heroes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_heroes) ([*Sơn Tinh* and *Thủy Tinh*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C6%A1n_Tinh_%E2%80%93_Th%E1%BB%A7y_Tinh)) which are referred to as a mountain and water spirit respectively and many other folklore tales.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcLeodThi_Dieu20018-484][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETranLe20175-501]

### Music

Main article: [Music of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Vietnam)

Three musicians are performing on a stage.

&#x20;*[Ca trù](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ca_tr%C3%B9)* trio performance in northern Vietnam

Traditional Vietnamese music varies between the country's northern and southern regions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETran1985-502] Northern classical music is Vietnam's oldest musical form and is traditionally more formal. The origins of Vietnamese classical opera (*[tuồng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu%E1%BB%93ng)*) can be traced to the Mongol invasions in the 13th century when the Vietnamese captured a [Chinese opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_opera) troupe.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiettinen1992163-503] Throughout its history, Vietnam has been the most heavily impacted by the [Chinese musical tradition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_China) along with those of Japan, [Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea) and [Mongolia](19271.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETran1985-502] *[Nhã nhạc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nh%C3%A3_nh%E1%BA%A1c)* is a refined form of imperial court music, *[chèo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%C3%A8o)* is a form of generally satirical [musical theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musical_theatre), while *[xẩm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X%E1%BA%A9m)* is a type of Vietnamese [folk music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_music). *[Quan họ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quan_h%E1%BB%8D)* (alternate singing) is popular in the [Bắc Ninh province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%E1%BA%AFc_Ninh_province). Another form of music called *[chầu văn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%E1%BA%A7u_v%C4%83n)* is used to invoke spirits during ceremonies. *[Nhạc dân tộc cải biên](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nh%E1%BA%A1c_d%C3%A2n_t%E1%BB%99c_c%E1%BA%A3i_bi%C3%AAn)* is a modern form of Vietnamese folk music which arose in the 1950s, while *[ca trù](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ca_tr%C3%B9)* is a complex form of sung poetry. *Hò* is a diverse genre of work songs performed in central and southern Vietnam. There is a range of traditional instruments, including the *[đàn bầu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%A0n_b%E1%BA%A7u)* (a monochord [zither](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zither)), the *[đàn gáo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%A0n_g%C3%A1o)* (a two-stringed [fiddle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiddle) with coconut body), and the *[đàn nguyệt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90%C3%A0n_nguy%E1%BB%87t)* (a two-stringed fretted moon [lute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lute)). In recent times, there have been some efforts at mixing Vietnamese traditional music—especially folk music—with modern music to revive and promote national music in the modern context and educate the younger generations about Vietnam's traditional musical instruments and singing styles.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVoice_of_Vietnam2018d-504] [Bolero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolero#Vietnam) music has gained popularity in the country since the 1930s, albeit with a different style—a combination of traditional Vietnamese music with Western elements.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuy2016-505] In the 21st century, the modern Vietnamese [pop music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music) industry known as [V-pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V-pop) incorporates elements of many popular genres worldwide, such as [electronic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_music), [dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_music) and [R\&B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_R\&B).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPhuong2018-506][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChen2018194-507]

### Media

Main article: [Media of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_Vietnam)

Shown is the logo of the state broadcaster Vietnam Television.

&#x20;[Vietnam Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_Television) (VTV), the main [state television](20803507.md) station

Vietnam's media sector is regulated by the government under the 2004 Law on Publication.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Justice''2004a-508] It is generally perceived that the country's media sector is controlled by the government and follows the official communist party line, though some newspapers are relatively outspoken.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEXuan_Dinh2000-509][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDattaMendizabal2018-510] The [Voice of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voice_of_Vietnam) (VOV) is the official state-run national radio broadcasting service, broadcasting internationally via shortwave using rented transmitters in other countries and providing broadcasts from its website, while [Vietnam Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_Television) (VTV) is the national television broadcasting company. Since 1997, Vietnam has regulated public [internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet) access extensively using both legal and technical means. The resulting lockdown is widely referred to as the "[Bamboo Firewall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_censorship_in_Vietnam)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkey2002-511] The collaborative project [OpenNet Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenNet_Initiative) classifies Vietnam's level of online political censorship to be "pervasive",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOpenNet_Initiative2012-512] while [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md) (RWB) considers Vietnam to be one of 15 global "internet enemies".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReporters_Without_Borders-513] Though the government of Vietnam maintains that such censorship is necessary to safeguard the country against obscene or sexually explicit content, many political and religious websites that are deemed to be undermining state authority are also blocked.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerkman_Klein_Center2006-514]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Vietnamese cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_cuisine)

A bowl of phở in Ho Chi Minh City.

A bowl of [phở](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ph%E1%BB%9F) in [Ho Chi Minh City](66956.md). Alongside with [bánh mì](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%A1nh_m%C3%AC), it is considered a [national dish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_dish) of Vietnam.

Traditionally, Vietnamese cuisine is based around [five fundamental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuxing_\(Chinese_philosophy\)) taste "elements" ([Vietnamese](32511.md): *ngũ vị*): spicy (metal), sour (wood), salty (water), bitter (fire), and sweet (earth).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Vietnam_Culture_Information_Network''2014-515] Common ingredients include [fish sauce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish_sauce), [shrimp paste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrimp_paste), [soy sauce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soy_sauce), rice, fresh [herbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herb), fruits, and [vegetables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetable). Vietnamese recipes use [lemongrass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cymbopogon), [ginger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ginger), [mint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mentha), [rau răm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rau_r%C4%83m), [long coriander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eryngium_foetidum), [Saigon cinnamon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saigon_cinnamon), [bird's eye chilli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bird's_eye_chilli), [lime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lime_\(fruit\)) and [basil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basil) leaves.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Special_Broadcasting_Service''2013-516]

Traditional Vietnamese cooking is known for its fresh ingredients, minimal use of oil, and reliance on herbs and vegetables; it is considered one of the healthiest cuisines worldwide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECorapi2010-517] The use of meats such as pork, beef, and chicken was relatively limited in the past. Instead freshwater fish, [crustaceans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crustacean) (particularly [crabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crab)), and [molluscs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mollusc) became widely used. Fish sauce, soy sauce, prawn sauce, and limes are among the main flavouring ingredients. Vietnam has a strong [street food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_food) culture, with 40 popular dishes commonly found throughout the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarkMiller2017-518] Many notable Vietnamese dishes such as *[gỏi cuốn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%E1%BB%8Fi_cu%E1%BB%91n)* (salad roll), *[bánh cuốn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%A1nh_cu%E1%BB%91n)* (rice noodle roll), *[bún riêu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%BAn_ri%C3%AAu)* (rice vermicelli soup) and *[phở](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ph%E1%BB%9F)* noodles originated in the north and were introduced to central and southern Vietnam by northern migrants.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENguyen2011-519][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThakerBarton2012170-520]

Local foods in the north are often less spicy than southern dishes, as the colder northern climate limits the production and availability of spices.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2017-521] [Black pepper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_pepper) is frequently used in place of [chillis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chilli_pepper) to produce spicy flavours.&#x20;

While drinks are usually served cold with [ice cubes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_cube), especially during the [summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer), hot drinks, such as [tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea), may be served to [treat illnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbal_medicine) and welcome guests. Some examples of basic Vietnamese drinks include *[cà phê đá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_iced_coffee)* (Vietnamese iced coffee), *[cà phê trứng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egg_coffee)* (egg coffee), *[chanh muối](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chanh_mu%E1%BB%91i)* (salted pickled lime juice), *[cơm rượu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C6%A1m_r%C6%B0%E1%BB%A3u)* (glutinous rice wine), *[nước mía](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Bc_m%C3%ADa)* (sugarcane juice) and *[trà sen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_lotus_tea)* (Vietnamese lotus tea).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBatruny2014-522]

### Holidays and festivals

Main articles: [Public holidays in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Vietnam) and [List of traditional festivals in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_traditional_festivals_in_Vietnam)

A large round white fabric band decorated with red figures and images for Tết

&#x20;Special *[Tết](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%E1%BA%BFt)* decoration in the country seen during the holiday

The country has eleven nationally recognised holidays. These include: [New Year's Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Year's_Day) on 1 January; lunisolar [Vietnamese New Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%E1%BA%BFt) (*Tết Nguyên Đán*) from the last day of the last lunar month to fifth day of the first [lunar month](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_calendar); [Hùng Kings' Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%B9ng_Kings'_Festival) on the 10th day of the third lunar month; [Reunification Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reunification_Day) on 30 April; [International Workers' Day](39353050.md) on 1 May; and [National Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Day_\(Vietnam\)) on 2 September.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Ministry_of_Justice''2012-523][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETravel201737-524][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELoan2018-525] During *Tết*, many Vietnamese from major cities return to their hometowns or villages for family reunions and to pray for dead ancestors.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnh2016b-526][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETrieu_Dan201792-527] Older people usually give the young a *[lì xì](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_envelope)* (red envelope), while special holiday food, such as *[bánh chưng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%A1nh_ch%C6%B0ng)* (rice cake) in a square shape together with variety of [dried fruits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dried_fruit), are presented in the house for visitors.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPike2018-528] Other festivals include *Tết Nguyên Tiêu*, *[Tết Trung Thu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%E1%BA%BFt_Trung_Thu)*, and various temple and nature festivals.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETravel201734-529] In the highlands, [Elephant Race Festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elephant_racing) are held annually in spring; riders will ride their elephants for about 1.6 km (0.99 mi) and the winning elephant will be given sugarcane.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEnglar200623-530] [Traditional Vietnamese weddings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_Vietnamese_wedding) remain popular.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndersonLee2005217-531]

### Sports

Main articles: [Sport in Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Vietnam) and [List of Vietnamese traditional games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Vietnamese_traditional_games)



[Mỹ Đình National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%E1%BB%B9_%C4%90%C3%ACnh_National_Stadium) in [Hanoi](56667.md)

The [Vovinam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vovinam), *Kim Kê* and *Võ Bình Định* are widespread in Vietnam,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreen2001548-532][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENghiaLuan2017-533] while [football](10568.md) is the country's most popular sport.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''FourFourTwo''2017-534] Its [national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_national_football_team) won the [ASEAN Football Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASEAN_Football_Championship) in [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_AFF_Championship), [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_AFF_Championship) and [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_ASEAN_Championship); and reached the [2007 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup), [quarter-finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_AFC_Asian_Cup_knockout_stage#Vietnam_vs_Japan) of [2019 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_AFC_Asian_Cup),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''China_Daily''2008-535][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''The_Saigon_Times_Daily''2018-536][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGomes2019-537] its junior team of [under-23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_national_under-23_football_team) became the runners-up of [2018 AFC U-23 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_AFC_U-23_Championship) and reached fourth place in [2018 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2018_Asian_Games), while the [under-20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_national_under-20_football_team) managed to qualify the [2017 FIFA U-20 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_FIFA_U-20_World_Cup) for the first time in their football history. And the [under-17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_national_under-17_football_team) achieved fourth place among 10 Teams of the [2000 AFC U-16 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_AFC_U-16_Championship).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERick2018-538][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYanJunLong2018-539] The [women's national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_women's_national_football_team) had first appearance at the [FIFA Women's World Cup](244862.md) in [2023](45271353.md), became the first 11-a-side national football team to participate in a World Cup tournament, and also traditionally dominates the [Southeast Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_Southeast_Asian_Games#Women's_tournament), along with its chief rival, [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand_women's_national_football_team). Other Western sports such as [badminton](3956.md), [tennis](29773.md), [volleyball](32558.md), [ping-pong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ping-pong) and [chess](5134.md) are also widely popular. Vietnam has participated in the [Summer Olympic Games](27566.md) since [1952](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_at_the_1952_Summer_Olympics). After the partition of the country in 1954, only South Vietnam competed in the games, sending athletes to the [1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_at_the_1956_Summer_Olympics) and [1972](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_at_the_1972_Summer_Olympics) Olympics. Since the reunification of Vietnam in 1976, it has competed as the Socialist Republic of Vietnam, attending every Summer Olympics from [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Summer_Olympics) onwards. The present Vietnam Olympic Committee was formed in 1976 and recognised by the [International Olympic Committee](15147.md) (IOC) in 1979.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternational_Olympic_Committee2018-540] Vietnam has never participated in the [Winter Olympic Games](34069.md). In 2016, Vietnam won its first [gold medal](526488.md) at the Olympics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESims2016-541] Basketball has become an increasingly popular sport in Vietnam, especially in [Ho Chi Minh City](66956.md), [Hanoi](56667.md) and [Can Tho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%E1%BA%A7n_Th%C6%A1) (formerly [Soc Trang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%B3c_Tr%C4%83ng_province)).[^cite_note-542] The country also held a national [multi-sport event](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-sport_event), the [Vietnam National Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_National_Games), held every four years.

[^cite_note-4]: &#x20;The most powerful political position is [General Secretary of the Communist Party of Vietnam](7482670.md). The general secretary leads the [Politburo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politburo_of_the_Communist_Party_of_Vietnam) and the [Secretariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Secretariat_of_the_Communist_Party_of_Vietnam), Vietnam's top decision-making bodies, making the officeholder the *de facto* leader of Vietnam.

[^cite_note-7]: &#x20;The area of Vietnam mentioned here is based on the land area statistics provided by the Vietnamese government. However, alternative figures exist. According to the CIA World Factbook, Vietnam's total area is 331,210 square kilometres,<sup>[\[1\]](#cite_note-Vietnam-1)</sup> while the BBC cites a slightly different measurement of 331,699 square kilometres.<sup>[\[5\]](#cite_note-Vietnam_country_profile-6)</sup>

[^cite_note-12]: &#x20;[Vietnamese](32511.md): *Việt Nam* [\[vîət nāːm\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Vietnamese) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/c/cb/Vietnam.ogg/Vietnam.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/c/cb/Vietnam.ogg/Vietnam.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>. The spelling "Viet Nam" is sometimes used in English by local and government-operated media. "Viet Nam" is, in fact, formally designated and recognised by the [Government of Vietnam](78640.md), the [United Nations](31769.md) and the [International Organization for Standardization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Organization_for_Standardization) as the standardised country name. See also [other spellings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_Vietnam#Other_spellings).

[^cite_note-13]: &#x20;[Vietnamese](32511.md): *Cộng hòa Xã hội chủ nghĩa Việt Nam*. Also seen with the alternate spelling as *Socialist Republic of Vietnam*, or *Socialist Republic of Việt Nam*, by Vietnamese orthography.

[^cite_note-14]: &#x20;The other one being [Laos](17752.md).

[^cite_note-23]: &#x20;According to Ye Wenxian, as cited by Wan, the ethnonym of the Yue in northwestern China is not associated with that of the Yue in southeastern China.<sup>[\[17\]](#cite_note-22)</sup>

[^cite_note-145]: &#x20;Neither the American government nor Ngô Đình Diệm's State of Vietnam signed anything at the 1954 Geneva Conference. The [State of Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Vietnam)'s delegation strenuously objected to any division of Vietnam; however, the French accepted the Việt Minh proposal<sup>[\[135\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGravel1971134-141)</sup> that Vietnam be united by elections under the supervision of "local commissions".<sup>[\[136\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGravel1971119-142)</sup> The United States, with the support of the State of Vietnam and the United Kingdom, countered with the "American Plan",<sup>[\[137\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGravel1971140-143)</sup> which provided for [United Nations](31769.md)-supervised unification elections. The plan, however, was rejected by [Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) and other communist delegations.<sup>[\[138\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKort201796-144)</sup>
